#2311. Signori e la Nazionale: «Quella ferita non si chiuderà mai», ritorno in azzurro

Il nuovo incarico nell’Under 20 allevia il dolore degli anni difficili; dopo la chiamata, il primo pensiero è andato a Tina.

Beppe Signori torna in azzurro: Malagò gli ha affidato il ruolo di capo delegazione della Nazionale Under 20. L’ex attaccante, che attendeva da tempo una chiamata nel calcio, si prepara al debutto del 12 novembre a Padova contro il Portogallo. Il nuovo incarico riporta con sé l’entusiasmo per una maglia indossata da protagonista e il ricordo degli anni segnati da una vicenda giudiziaria che ha condizionato profondamente la sua vita.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

In vista della partita contro il Portogallo, Signori descrive sensazioni diverse da quelle vissute da calciatore: «È un debutto diverso, ma per certi versi molto più emozionante. Sono carico di entusiasmo, sento una grande responsabilità».

Alla proposta di Malagò, racconta, non ha mai pensato di rispondere negativamente. La considerava un’opportunità attesa a lungo e non avrebbe voluto rinunciarvi per orgoglio o per la rabbia legata a ciò che aveva subito: «Assolutamente mai! Era una grande opportunità, l'aspettavo da tanto».

Signori, intervistato da Il Resto del Carlino, vuole partire da determinazione, sacrificio e passione. Intende aiutare i giovani a comprendere la responsabilità di rappresentare la propria nazione e l’orgoglio di vestire l’azzurro. A suo giudizio, oggi i ragazzi sono abituati ad avere tutto, mentre la sua generazione doveva conquistarsi ogni cosa.

Alla domanda se l’azzurro abbia perso fascino dopo tre Mondiali mancati, risponde senza esitazioni: «No, l'azzurro non passa mai. Quella maglia ti dà una sensazione forte, unica». Ricorda la competizione per un posto ai suoi tempi, quando tra gli attaccanti c’erano anche Baggio, Zola, Mancini, Vialli e Casiraghi. Per questo ritiene che i giovani debbano riconoscere il privilegio di giocare per l’Italia e dimostrare di esserne all’altezza, nonostante le distrazioni esterne e una società orientata, secondo lui, al tutto e subito.

#2307. Derek McInnes makes Rangers transfer stance crystal clear over in-form star

Derek McInnes hopes Rangers can turn James Penrice’s loan move into a permanent deal after making his feelings clear to the Ibrox hierarchy.

The 27-year-old is currently on loan at Rangers from Greek side AEK Athens and has impressed since making the move to Glasgow.

Penrice’s form has now been rewarded with his first Scotland call-up, while McInnes has made no secret of his desire to keep the defender beyond his current agreement.

Asked whether discussions had taken place over making the move permanent, McInnes said: “No, I think, well, certainly I've made my feelings clear on it. Ideally, I would like to have done it as a permanent, but the best way forward at the time was a loan.

“But I think we're all aware of what it needs to be to make it permanent. But I think there's a lot of goodwill all round to try and that could be something that we'd like to do.

“The conversations I've had with Jim and the board have been along those lines, and hopefully we can make that permanent.”

Rangers-daft pundit sounds Celtic alarm with ‘Covid year’ warning

Your 'loud and crazy' supporters won't stop us winning at Celtic Park

Furious Redknapp demands ex Rangers transfer chief is axed for transfer blunders

Penrice has quickly established himself as an important figure under McInnes, with the Rangers boss impressed by the quality and energy he has brought to the side.

He continued: “Because I think what he's done since the first minute is just play with so much enthusiasm, clearly wants to be here.

“Plays properly and he brings quality, but he also brings energy, the fans take to him because of how he performs and how much he wants to win.

“So it's just for me, those types of boys, that age is actually quite ironic. He's one of the older ones at 27, I think. And a lot of these things are happening for him now, the first Scotland call-up and all the rest of it.

“But he's a player that I trust and I think the supporters trust him.

“So hopefully we can make it a longer-term thing going forward. But that's for the people upstairs to try and sort out.”

#2305. Non vedremo più Ronaldo con la maglia del Portogallo: CR7 ha dato l'addio alla Nazionale

Probabilmente se oggi un bambino in giro per il mondo ha una maglia del Portogallo, quella è di Cristiano Ronaldo. Dopo 23 anni di Nazionale, giovedì 5 novembre CR7 ha dato l'addio a quella che è stata casa sua per più di un'era calcistica. Sicuramente non lo ha fatto nel migliore dei modi perché l'ultima immagine che si ha di lui è il ritiro abbandonato dopo lo scontro con Jorge Jesus.

Per spiegare cos'è stato Cristiano Ronaldo ci vorrebbe molto più di un articolo, probabilmente non basterebbe neanche un libro, ma c'è una parola che lo descrivo meglio di qualunque altra e quella è gol. L'eterno fenomeno con la numero 7 sulle spalle è il calciatore ad averne segnati più di tutti (146!) con le nazionali e ha trascinato un Paese a 3 trionfi, quando prima di lui nessun altro c'era riuscito.

Ronaldo si è congedato con un lungo messaggio: "È arrivato il giorno di restituire la maglia numero 7 della nostra nazionale, quella di tutti noi. Non l’ho mai considerata esclusivamente mia, ma per 23 anni ho avuto il privilegio e la responsabilità di indossarla, difenderla e portarla con me negli stadi di tutto il mondo.

Oggi è arrivato il momento di consegnarla al futuro. Non è stata una decisione facile, perché alcune scelte non si prendono soltanto con la testa, ma anche con il cuore. Si conclude un percorso iniziato nell’agosto 2003, un percorso che ha cambiato la mia vita per sempre. Quando guardo indietro e penso a tutto quello che ho vissuto con la nazionale, non vedo soltanto partite, gol, record o trofei. Vedo persone, immigrati che, per 90 minuti, si sentivano un po’ più vicini al Portogallo. Vedo milioni di portoghesi che non ho mai incontrato personalmente, ma che hanno fatto parte della mia vita. Perché alla fine il calcio è molto più di un semplice gioco. Il calcio sono le persone.

Durante tutta la mia carriera in nazionale ho sempre cercato di superare i miei limiti. Volevo vincere, volevo essere il migliore, volevo fare la storia. Sono sempre stato esigente con me stesso, ho sempre voluto di più, ho sempre creduto che potessimo arrivare ancora più lontano. E ho dato tutto per questa maglia.

Ci sono stati pomeriggi e serate perfette, vittorie che non dimenticherò mai e sconfitte il cui ricordo ancora oggi mi fa male. Ho anche fallito, ho anche sofferto. Ci sono stati momenti in cui non sono riuscito a dare ai portoghesi ciò che desideravano così tanto. Ma non mi sono mai arreso. E soprattutto non ho mai smesso di essere orgoglioso di rappresentare il Portogallo.

Nel corso di questi anni ho imparato cose molto semplici: i gol passano, i trofei restano nei musei, i record prima o poi vengono battuti. Ma c’è qualcosa che il tempo non potrà mai cancellare: il posto che occupiamo nel cuore delle persone. Se vi ho mai reso felici, se uno dei miei gol vi ha mai fatto abbracciare qualcuno, dimenticare una preoccupazione o provare orgoglio per il nostro Paese, allora tutto questo è valso la pena.

Perché essere portoghese significa anche questo. Soffriamo insieme, ci rialziamo insieme. E torniamo a crederci. Il Portogallo avrà sempre bisogno di giovani che sognano, di giocatori che non si arrendono mai, di nuove generazioni che credono sia possibile arrivare dove nessuno è mai arrivato prima. Perché un Paese che continua a far nascere nuovi eroi è un Paese che non smette mai di credere in sé stesso.

Ai miei compagni di nazionale voglio dedicare un messaggio speciale. Grazie per ogni corsa, per ogni abbraccio, per ogni discussione, per ogni silenzio prima di entrare in campo, per ogni vittoria che abbiamo festeggiato insieme e per ogni sconfitta che ci ha reso più forti. Ho avuto il privilegio di essere il vostro capitano, ma un capitano non cammina mai da solo. E voi fate parte della mia storia.

A tutti gli allenatori, dirigenti, medici, fisioterapisti, magazzinieri, collaboratori e a tutte le persone che hanno lavorato al mio fianco nel corso di questi anni, rivolgo il mio grazie. Molti di voi non compaiono mai nelle fotografie. Ma senza di voi nessuna di quelle fotografie sarebbe mai stata possibile. Alla mia famiglia, grazie per aver condiviso questo sogno con me. Grazie per aver compreso le mie assenze, i sacrifici e i momenti in cui la nazionale ha dovuto occupare uno spazio che apparteneva anche a voi. Se sono arrivato così lontano è perché abbiamo sempre camminato mano nella mano.

E a voi, portoghesi, a tutti i portoghesi sparsi negli angoli più lontani del mondo, voglio dire un’ultima cosa. C’è un’immagine che porterò sempre con me: il momento in cui risuona l’inno nazionale. Guardo intorno a me, vedo i miei compagni, vedo le tribune, vedo bambini, madri, padri e nonni. Vedo portoghesi che hanno percorso migliaia di chilometri soltanto per essere lì. E durante quei pochi minuti smettiamo di essere diversi. Non ci sono più club, non ci sono più colori, non ci sono più classi sociali, non ci sono più professioni, non ci sono più opinioni. Esiste soltanto una passione: il Portogallo.

È questo sentimento che porterò con me per il resto della mia vita. Oggi metto fine al mio percorso sul campo con la nazionale, me ne vado a testa alta, orgoglioso del cammino compiuto e con la consapevolezza di aver dato tutto per il Portogallo. Ma non smetterò mai di essere uno di voi. Continuerò a soffrire, a emozionarmi e a festeggiare ogni vittoria del Portogallo insieme a voi. Ho dedicato la mia vita a lavorare per diventare il miglior giocatore del mondo.

Oggi capisco che la mia più grande vittoria è stata avere il privilegio di rappresentare il miglior Paese del mondo. Grazie per questi 23 anni. Grazie per aver creduto in me. Grazie per avermi permesso di vivere un sogno iniziato quando ero ancora un bambino, a Madeira, mentre correvo dietro a un pallone. La maglia numero 7 resta, l’orgoglio di averla indossata mi apparterrà per sempre. Il Portogallo sarà sempre dentro di me. Per sempre".

#2301. Atalanta, solo 1-1 con il Kairat Almaty. Sarri: "Difficile reagire con 14 giocatori fuori"

L'Atalanta evita la figuraccia completa, ma non esce dalla crisi: solo 1-1 con il Kairat Almaty alla New Balance Arena, in Conference League. Vittoria ancora rimandata per Sarri, che non trova il successo dallo scorso 15 ottobre e non riesce a fermare l'emorragia. Solo l'ingresso di Krstovic evita di farci parlare di un'altra sconfitta contro la modesta squadra kazaka, che al di là della sofferenza nel finale non ruba nulla, a Bergamo.

Nonostante il momento delicato, Sarri non può fare a meno di cercare di far rifiatare qualche big. Fuori De Ketelaere, Krstovic, Ederson e Zappacosta. Davanti c'è Scamacca, con Rowe, Samardzic e Sulemana a supporto, in mezzo Elmas e Pasalic. Esordio dal primo minuto per il 19enne Idele come terzino destro.

Dopo due guizzi di Rowe che va vicino al gol in avvio (di destro e di testa) l'Atalanta ha qualche difficoltà nel trovare spazi. Ci pensa il Kairat a mettersi in difficoltà da solo: Kassabulat rischia l'autogol su retropassaggio per Anarbekov. Bisogna attendere il 26' per altri squilli: Rowe riceve da Bernasconi, destro a giro dal limite parato da Anarbekov. C'è anche il Kairat: Carnesecchi è attento e reattivo sui tentativi di Guel e Mrynskiy. Pasalic riceve da Elmas, ma solo davanti al portiere mette incredibilmente fuori.

Nella ripresa Sarri cambia: dentro Ederson per uno spento Sulemana. Doccia fredda al 53': Samardzic perde palla, Edmilson imbuca e Guel fa 0-1 con la difesa di Sarri che lascia un'autostrada. Sarri cambia subito proprio Samardzic e Rowe inserendo De Ketelaere e Zalewski, che fa partire l'azione dalla quale arriva il tiro al volo di Idele, che poteva fare meglio.

Elmas pericoloso due volte, nel primo caso segna pure sull'assist di Zalewski, ma in fuorigioco. Entra anche Krstovic per uno Scamacca anonimo ed è proprio l'ex Lecce a favorire la girata di Ederson all'80', ma Anarbekov gli dice no. Sul corner successivo il portiere kazako esce male: sponda di Obric e Krstovic fa 1-1. Assalto nel finale della Dea, più con il cuore che con le idee: ci prova Pasalic da fuori, ma manda alto. Finisce 1-1 fra i fischi dello stadio.

Ai microfoni di Sky Sport, al termine del match, interviene l'allenatore dell'Atalanta, Maurizio Sarri. Ecco le sue parole "Che cosa non ha funzionato? Se in un momento in cui hai fuori 14 giocatori questa deve essere la settimana di rilancio, la vedo dura. Oggi avevamo 10 infortunati e quattro giocatori con valutazioni che sconsigliavano l'utilizzo per più di mezz'ora, quindi diventa difficile. Potevamo vincerla sicuramente se non prendevamo un gol stupido, poi dopo la partita si è fatta difficile. Meno male che non abbiamo mollato, perlomeno dal punto di vista della voglia di portare in fondo un risultato positivo. Abbiamo fatto fatica: la circolazione della palla è stata piuttosto lenta e abbiamo faticato ad attaccare gli spazi e a giocare contro una squadra chiusa. Però, con tantissime attenuanti, prendiamo questo punto e vediamo di andare a conquistare la qualificazione più avanti, quando staremo meglio sotto tutti i punti di vista".

#2297. Ronaldo, ritiro dal Portogallo: «È arrivato il momento»

Jorge Jesus aveva riaperto le porte al numero 7: resta l’ipotesi di un ultimo saluto nella prossima pausa.

Secondo quanto riportato da Onefootball, Cristiano Ronaldo dice addio alla Nazionale portoghese. L’annuncio del ritiro è arrivato attraverso un video pubblicato sul suo profilo Instagram, nel quale ha ripercorso gli oltre vent’anni trascorsi con la maglia del Portogallo.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

La decisione segue di poche settimane quella, definita storica, del suo rivale Lionel Messi. Per Ronaldo si chiude un percorso con la Nazionale segnato da 146 gol in 234 partite.

Nel video, l’ex giocatore del Real Madrid ripercorre il tempo trascorso a rappresentare il proprio Paese. «È arrivato il momento», è la frase attribuita a Ronaldo in un titolo collegato all’annuncio.

Un altro titolo dedicato al ritiro riporta le parole «Consegno la 7 della Nazionale dopo 23 anni». Il riferimento è alla maglia numero 7 indossata da Cristiano con il Portogallo.

I 146 gol in 234 presenze hanno accompagnato il Portogallo alla conquista di un Europeo e di due Nations League. Nel racconto di Onefootball, resta il rammarico per il Mondiale: Ronaldo ha partecipato a sei edizioni senza riuscire a vincerlo.

L’addio alla Nazionale arriva dunque dopo oltre due decenni e dopo i successi ottenuti con la selezione portoghese, ma senza il titolo mondiale.

#2294. Martin O'Neill opens up on Celtic fan meeting and denies ulterior motive

Martin O’Neill has opened up on his decision to face Celtic supporters at a recent meeting in Greenock and insists the questions fired at the club were “fair”.

The Hoops manager attended the gathering to hear first-hand the concerns of fans amid growing discontent over the running of the club and recent results.

O’Neill revealed he only became aware of the meeting at the “11th hour”, but was determined to attend and get a better understanding of the mood among supporters.

“I just wanted to go down to it and essentially experience what the supporters are feeling and see how we can do something about it,” he said.

Asked about the main grievances raised during the meeting, O’Neill suggested there was little unexpected about the issues put to those representing Celtic.

“I think it's straightforward,” he said. “I think you would know what they would be.

“I think they were fair, absolutely. And I think that the board as a whole tried to answer them as honestly as they possibly could.”

Real Madrid welcomes sporting royalty as tennis icon joins board of directors Scotland on alert as Slovenia rocked by fan mutiny before crunch Nations League clash

O’Neill also insisted the strength of feeling expressed by supporters did not come as a surprise.

“No, I don't think anything really surprised me at all,” he said.

“I think that the questions that they were asking, I think that the board would have been expecting those questions to be asked and obviously hoping to answer them in the best possible manner, or as honestly as they could.”

The Celtic boss revealed it was not the first time he had attended such an event, having gone along to a similar gathering several months ago, and stressed he would be prepared to meet supporters again if his schedule permits.

However, O’Neill dismissed the suggestion that attending the Greenock meeting alongside club representatives could be viewed as an attempt to shore up his own position.

“My job doesn't depend on going to meetings like that,” he said. “My job depends on results. It really does, and I cannot forget that.

“If I am the manager and the next meeting comes up and time allows, I don't mind going to it.”

#2291. La Juventus è in crescita e in Europa League è sempre bella: ora obiettivo ottavi

La Juventus continua a crescere e mette un altro tassello nella costruzione della nuova identità targata Luciano Spalletti. Il successo per 2-1 sul campo dell'AZ Alkmaar, firmato dalle reti di Teun Koopmeiners e Randal Kolo Muani, vale la terza vittoria nelle prime quattro giornate di Europa League e il quarto posto momentaneo nella classifica generale. Un piazzamento che, se confermato al termine della League Phase, garantirebbe l'accesso diretto agli ottavi di finale, evitando il passaggio dai playoff. La Vecchia Signora ha mostrato personalità e qualità per oltre un'ora, prima di complicarsi la vita nel finale: il gol di Meerdink all'88' e il pareggio annullato agli olandesi nel recupero hanno trasformato una vittoria apparentemente tranquilla in una sofferenza fino all'ultimo secondo.

Nel dopogara ai microfoni di Sky Sport, Spalletti ha rivendicato con forza il valore della prestazione, sottolineando anche il livello degli avversari: "Sono soddisfattissimo. Hanno giocato una partita seria contro una squadra fortissima. Stasera abbiamo fatto una grande partita, contro un grande avversario". Il tecnico ha evidenziato soprattutto la capacità dei suoi giocatori di affrontare l'AZ senza rinunciare alla propria proposta di gioco, accettando i duelli individuali e cercando di imporre qualità e personalità anche in trasferta. "Quando si fanno partite così, in un campo come questo, in cui non perdevano da 17 partite, è segno che vieni con autorità e qualità a giocarti la partita", ha aggiunto l'allenatore bianconero. Un riconoscimento rivolto all'intero gruppo, compresi i giocatori subentrati, per l'atteggiamento mostrato in una serata particolarmente impegnativa.

La soddisfazione per il risultato non cancella però le preoccupazioni legate alla gestione degli ultimi minuti. Spalletti non ha nascosto il proprio disappunto per l'atteggiamento troppo rinunciatario della squadra, incapace di sfruttare gli spazi concessi dall'AZ per chiudere definitivamente la partita: "Ci siamo messi lì ad aspettare, abbiamo preso gol. Se fosse durata altri cinque minuti secondo me non l'avremmo portata a casa. Andava fatto il terzo gol, non bisognava reggere". Il tecnico ha poi individuato nella mancanza di decisione uno dei principali problemi emersi nel finale: "Mi preoccupa come abbiamo preso gol, per una decisione non presa. Le decisioni non prese sono peggiori di quelle sbagliate". Resta comunque il valore di una vittoria ottenuta su un campo dove l'AZ non perdeva da 17 partite. La Juventus porta a casa tre punti fondamentali per la qualificazione diretta agli ottavi e conferma i progressi delle ultime settimane. Il percorso di Spalletti comincia a prendere forma, ma il messaggio dell'allenatore è chiaro: per diventare una squadra davvero matura non basta saper costruire il vantaggio, bisogna anche imparare a gestirlo senza smettere di giocare.

#2288. Sheffield United man to attract transfer ‘interest’ in January window, says reporter

Sheffield United could face a battle to keep hold of the England youth international in the upcoming January transfer window amid potential attention from other clubs. He is under contract at SUFC until June 2028 meaning he still has just under two years left to run down on his deal at Bramall Lane.

Peck, 22, has become a key player for the Blades in the Championship under the guidance of head coach Chris Wilder. He has made 16 appearances for the South Yorkshire outfit in all competitions this season, 14 of which have come in the league, and he has chipped in with four goals and three assists.

Oxley has shared this update on his situation, as per a report by BBC Sport: “Peck’s stock is high, and rising. He has become a regular in the England under-21 side, he’s one of the first names on the Blades team sheet, and his underlying shooting, passing, possession and defending statistics show he ranks as one of the Championship’s best players in his position.

“He was coveted by Premier League clubs last summer, and you’d imagine he will be the subject of interest once again in January, especially after United sold a trio of stars pre-season to help balance the books.”

Sheffield United won’t want to lose Peck anytime soon, especially halfway through this campaign. He is a valuable member of SUFC’s squad and has the capacity to grow and develop even further down the line.

West Ham were said to be keen on him in the summer after their relegation from the Premier League. However, nothing materialised in the end and he stayed put beyond the last deadline in September.

Peck, who is from Worthing, has enjoyed plenty of game time with the Blades over recent times. He battles it out with the likes of Kalvin Phillips, Oli Arblaster and Joe Rothwell, among others, for minutes in Wilder’s starting XI.

He joined Sheffield United as a youngster back in 2021 after rising up through the academy at Arsenal. He has since played over 100 matches and was also loaned out to Oldham Athletic back in 2022 to get some more experience under his belt down in League Two.

Sheffield United’s chances of retaining the services of Peck in the long run will likely depend on whether they can reach the top flight. If they go up this term, he would probably stick around at SUFC.

If they stay in the second tier though, it would be a surprise if he stayed beyond next year. The Blades need to take advantage of his ability whilst they can.

Wilder’s men have been in good form recently and are genuine contenders for the play-offs if they can stay consistent. They beat Preston North End 3-2 last time out on Tuesday night with Peck scoring a hat-trick at Deepdale against the Lilywhites.

Sheffield United are now unbeaten in their last four and they are starting to build a bit of momentum now as they aim to get into the top eight. They are sat in 9th position in the table and they are only a point behind West Ham in 8th ahead of their upcoming match against Charlton Athletic away.

#2285. The Celtic youngster Martin O'Neill was keen to check on during Ross County clash

Martin O'Neill has namechecked one Celtic youngster in particular he was keen to watch when taking in the B team's clash with Ross County.

The Parkhead side's academy players took on the League One side in the KDM Evoluton Trophy, losing 2-0. First teamers, and summer arrivals, Landon Emenalo, Shim Mheuka and Joel van den Berg.

O'Neill wanted to check on the trio's performances but was also keen to check out the young players, in particular midfielder Liam Kennedy.

The 17-year-old has played 38 minutes for East Kilbride as part of a cooperation loan agreement and played 90 minutes for Celtic B last night.

When Yang could make Celtic return as O'Neill provides update after training return Martin O'Neill opens up on Celtic fan meeting and denies ulterior motive

Asked about his appearance in Dingwall, O'Neill said: "Well, in my first spell years ago, I don't remember going to too many Celtic meetings, but then I'm not so sure that we had supposedly that set of crises at the time, if that's what you can call it. But yes, I am actually or used to be a very, very hands-on manager.

"But I wanted to see the players, primarily the players. First of all, there's a couple of young lads who are trying to come through, like young Liam Kennedy, lads like that there who I think have a chance. They're young and also we had Joel van den Berg playing a game after injury and the two young lads from Chelsea who haven't played that much football even since summer time.

"So it was important for us and I thought the opposition were going to be very strong, which they were, and I have to say I thought Ross County played really well, really well to win the game and deserved to win it. But it was great to see the game."

#2284. Blackburn Rovers vs West Ham predictions: Scott Parker backed for first win in ‘entertaining contest’

Blackburn Rovers head into Saturday’s clash with West Ham sitting 14th in the Championship table. Tony Mowbray’s side have endured an inconsistent start to the campaign, collecting 18 points from 14 games, with five wins, three draws and six defeats leaving them just outside the Championship play-off picture.

Rovers have shown signs of their attacking potential, though, beating Wrexham 2-0 at Ewood Park two weeks ago. Andri Gudjohnsen scored both goals in that victory, taking his league tally to nine and accounting for almost half of Blackburn’s goals this season.

West Ham arrive currently 8th in the Championship with 22 points and have been among the more prolific sides in the league, averaging more than 2.5 goals per game. Scott Parker, appointed following Nuno Espirito Santo’s departure, will be looking for a response after a disappointing result last time out.

WHUFC went three goals down against Cardiff City before eventually rescuing a 3-3 draw. Divine Mukasa, Arne Engels and Max Kilman were all on target in South Wales, but Parker will be concerned by his side’s defensive shortcomings as they prepare for another difficult away assignment.

“Blackburn Rovers have shown enough attacking quality to suggest they can cause West Ham problems, particularly with Gudjohnsen enjoying an excellent run in front of goal. The Icelandic striker has become an important figure for Mowbray, while Todd Cantwell’s creativity gives the hosts another route to hurting their opponents.

“However, West Ham possess considerably more individual quality and should have enough firepower to trouble Rovers’ defence. Jarrod Bowen has been particularly influential, registering seven goals and seven assists in 14 league appearances.

“The visitors will need to improve defensively after their collapse against Cardiff, but their attacking options should ultimately make the difference. Blackburn can contribute to an entertaining contest at Ewood Park, although I expect Parker’s side to come away with all three points.”

“Blackburn Rovers have had some tough games of late. They were narrowly beaten by a great West Brom side, thrashed by Swansea City, and then hard done by to lose to Stoke City after a very soft disallowed goal late on.

“I think Rovers are going to see form come and go this season, largely because their squad isn’t very deep and relies on the form and availability of key figures like Andri Gudjohnsen, Todd Cantwell and Sam Morsy. Despite this, they’re dangerous with their front-footed play under Tony Mowbray and have had some good results at home.

“West Ham will be one to keep an eye on this weekend though. They will have had a couple of days to work under Scott Parker on the training ground after that great comeback draw at Cardiff City.

“We’ll get a better idea of what to expect from WHUFC as they settle into Parker’s management. I do think they have the talent to win the midfield battle though, and with quality players like Jarrod Bowen, I can see them creating the chances to win this one.”

#2281. Rambaudi avvisa la Lazio: “Non devi andare a Napoli e pensare di gestire la partita”

Le analisi e i consigli di Roberto Rambaudi in vista del big match di Serie A fra Lazio e Napoli

In vista della sfida contro il Napoli, l'ex giocatore della Lazio Roberto Rambaudi ha analizzato a Radiosei la pesante assenza di Mattia Zaccagni e l'approccio tattico e mentale con cui la squadra capitolina dovrà affrontare il match al Maradona.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Zaccagni è quello che salta l'uomo, fa assist, crea superiorità; sarà un test per chi prende il suo posto. Ad ogni modo, nonostante il Napoli sia in difficoltà, la vittoria non è mai scontata. Sono forti ma non sono nella condizione mentale ottimale. Immagino la squadra verrà contestata; alcuni giocatori, i più importanti e rappresentativi, proveranno a dare una scossa, in casa, prima della sosta. Non devi andare a Napoli e pensare di gestire la partita, credo che molto si deciderà nei primi 20-25', se li fai galvanizzare o se li metti subito in difficoltà, creando dall'inizio.

Se Tavares e Pedraza stanno bene, potrebbe essere un'opzione vedere loro due dal 1' a sinistra. Credo sia una scelta che Gattuso farà in base a come vede i calciatori durante la settimana. Magari anche Cancellieri a sinistra può essere una soluzione, io Isaksen da destra non lo toglierei.

#2280. Celtic treble winner formally announces bid to become FIFA president

Former Celtic defender Ramon Vega has formally announced his candidacy to become the next president of FIFA.

The ex-Switzerland international confirmed that he intends to stand in the FIFA presidential election scheduled for March 18, 2027 in Rabat, Morocco.

Vega, who enjoyed a memorable spell at Celtic during the 2000/01 Treble-winning campaign under Martin O'Neill, launched his bid with a strongly worded statement calling for major changes to the way world football is governed.

The 55-year-old declared that "football belongs to the people" as he outlined his desire to restore trust in the sport's governing body.

Vega said: "After more than a decade of preparation, I formally announce my candidacy for the Presidency of FIFA in 2027.

"I bring the mentality of a player, the discipline of a defender and decades of experience from the pitch to the boardroom.

How Celtic trio fared in B Team defeat to Ross County as O'Neill watches on Joel van den Berg recovery latest as three Celtic players set for B Team duty

"I defended my team on the field. Now I am ready to defend football itself, its integrity, its people and its future."

Vega also insisted his campaign was not motivated by "personal ambition, power or privilege", describing his candidacy as a push for reconciliation and fundamental change within world football.

He added: "No individual is greater than football. No institution should ever place itself above the game.

"This is not merely an announcement of candidacy. It is a call for fundamental change, a commitment to reconciliation and a vision for a new era of world football."

📣 OFFICIAL ANNOUNCEMENT I FORMALLY ANNOUNCE MY CANDIDACY FOR THE FIFA PRESIDENTIAL ELECTION — 18 MARCH 2027 RABAT, MOROCCO FOOTBALL BELONGS TO THE PEOPLE — NOT TO THOSE WHO CONTROL IT. Football stands at a defining crossroads. The decisions we make today will shape the future of our beautiful game for generations to come. WE DECLARE ZERO TOLERANCE for leadership that has humiliated the passion of five billion football fans around the world and insulted the dignity, dedication and integrity of the millions of people who devote their lives to our beautiful game. Football belongs to its people. Their passion deserves respect, their voices deserve to be heard, and their contributions deserve recognition — never humiliation, intimidation or contempt. After more than a decade of preparation, I formally announce my candidacy for the Presidency of FIFA in 2027. Alongside me stands my Hungarian wife and First Lady, Claudia — a pioneer with over 25 years of experience in professional men s football. Together, we share an unwavering commitment to restoring trust, reforming governance and reuniting the global football family. I bring the mentality of a player, the discipline of a defender and decades of experience from the pitch to the boardroom. I defended my team on the field. Now I am ready to defend football itself — its integrity, its people and its future. My candidacy is not driven by personal ambition, power or privilege. It is driven by responsibility, conviction and a profound love for the game that has given me everything. I AM NOT STANDING TO CONTINUE THE WAR. I AM STANDING TO END IT. No individual is greater than football. No institution should ever place itself above the game. This is not merely an announcement of candidacy. It is a call for fundamental change, a commitment to reconciliation and a vision for a new era of world football. I invite every member association, player, coach, referee and supporter across the world to read my full official presidential announcement attached below. 👇 Read it. Reflect on it. Challenge its ideas. Above all, join the conversation about the future of the game we all love. Vega remains well remembered by the Celtic support despite spending only half a season in Glasgow.

He joined O'Neill's side on loan from Tottenham in December 2000 and played his part in the Hoops completing a domestic clean sweep that season, including scoring twice in an Old Firm victory over Rangers.

The former defender has now set his sights on one of the biggest positions in the game as he prepares to challenge for the FIFA presidency in 2027.

#2279. ‘Catalyst’ victory can inspire Wrexham to a result in Wolves clash: Predictions

Wrexham beat league leaders Middlesbrough at home on Wednesday night. George Thomason and Ollie Rathbone scored two late goals for the Welsh outfit as they returned to winning ways in impressive fashion.

The Red Dragons are sat in 16th place in the Championship table after their first 14 fixtures and they have made a slow start to the season. They are only six points off the play-offs now behind West Ham in 8th position.

As for Wolves, they came from behind away at Bristol City last time out to win 2-1. Newcastle United loan man Sean Neave scored two second-half goals to earn WWFC an important three points at Ashton Gate against the Robins.

Cesar Peixoto‘s men have now risen into 2nd in the division behind Middlesbrough ahead of Birmingham City. The Midlands club were relegated from the Premier League earlier this year after they finished in the bottom three along with West Ham and Burnley.

“Wrexham’s win over Middlesbrough was much needed and has eased some pressure off Phil Parkinson. It has been a tough season for the Red Dragons so far and they will be eager to hit some form now.

“The Welsh side haven’t scored enough goals in this campaign and are lacking a cutting edge. In fact, only six teams have scored less than them in the Championship so far.

“Wolves found a way to win at Bristol City which is a good sign for a team eyeing automatic promotion. WWFC are now unbeaten in their last five games since their home defeat to Bolton Wanderers last month.

“Cesar Peixoto’s squad is littered with Premier League quality and they will fancy their chances in North Wales. However, I think it may end a high scoring draw with the hosts seemingly back on track now.”

“Wrexham produced arguably their best performance of the season to beat Middlesbrough on Wednesday night. That has to be a catalyst for Phil Parkinson if he’s to hold onto his job and if the Red Dragons are to push up the Championship table.

“Another tricky game awaits here with Wolves unbeaten in five, but another home match marks a chance for the North Welsh side to build some momentum and head into the break on a high.

“Wolves are in high spirits themselves though. They’ve responded to poor form with a five-game unbeaten run, the most recent being an impressive comeback win thanks to a Sean Neave brace. That will be a real confidence boost for the young striker as well, as he looks to step up to the senior game and deliver while Wanderers star Raul Jimenez is out.

“This is a tough one to call, as Wrexham are capable of picking up another win if they play like they did on Wednesday. However, I can’t see Wolves losing, so I’ll say this ends level.”

#2277. Derek McInnes on Findlay Curtis Rangers future as he reveals player stance

Derek McInnes has made it clear Rangers want Findlay Curtis to commit his long-term future to the club, with the youngster also determined to remain at Ibrox and win trophies.

The 20-year-old is out of contract in the summer and faces stiff competition for regular starts in McInnes' side.

However, the Rangers boss has been impressed with Curtis' attitude and contribution and believes the academy graduate is firmly on the path towards establishing himself as a first-team regular.

"He knows how much we want him to be here longer term," said McInnes. "The club have made that clear to him as well.

"And Findlay's telling me that he wants to be here. He wants to win trophies here. This is his club.

"He knows what we think about him. I'm not here to say to him, 'be patient', because I want him to be in a hurry to get more starts.

"But I believe that he is well on his way to being a good Rangers player going forward."

The Celtic youngster Martin O'Neill was keen to check on during Ross County clash Real Madrid welcomes sporting royalty as tennis icon joins board of directors

McInnes insists there is no shame in Curtis having to battle for minutes given the competition for places at Ibrox, but has challenged the youngster to make the most of every opportunity.

"There's no shame in getting into this Rangers team week in, week out," he said. "He's got good competition there.

"For any player, whether it's homegrown or not, they've got to accept that at Rangers there should be competition for places.

"You don't waste your minutes. If you're in the team, do your best to stay in the team.

"Findlay has contributed really well for us, really strongly. He is a good trainer. He's a brilliant kid."

McInnes also revealed he has enjoyed working with Curtis since taking charge and highlighted the importance of the minutes he has already accumulated, whether from the start or off the bench.

"Getting those minutes is vitally important," he added. "Hopefully this season he can get the minutes that he's happy with, but we're certainly happy with a lot of his contributions."

#2276. Panchina Napoli, idea Conceição: la sfida con la Lazio è un dentro o fuori per Allegri

In attesa del dentro o fuori contro la Lazio, la panchina azzurra valuta il profilo di Sergio Conceição come possibile erede.

La pesante sconfitta subita contro il Porto in Champions League e le difficoltà iniziali della stagione hanno fatto scattare i primi dubbi sulla permanenza di Massimiliano Allegri, con indiscrezioni provenienti direttamente dalla Francia precisamente da Foot Mercato, che accostano con insistenza la panchina del Napoli a Sergio Conceição. Questa voce di mercato indica inoltre che la prossima sfida di campionato contro la Lazio rappresenterà un vero e proprio dentro o fuori per il futuro di Allegri.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il profilo del tecnico lusitano non rappresenta una novità per la dirigenza partenopea, che aveva già tentato di ingaggiarlo nel 2021 e nel 2023. In entrambe le occasioni, però, la trattativa era sfumata a causa dell'alta richiesta economica del Porto per la clausola rescissoria. Oggi lo scenario è completamente differente, dato che l'allenatore ha guidato il Milan nella seconda parte della stagione 2024/25 ed è attualmente svincolato, pronto a firmare subito per una nuova squadra.

Forte del recente rifiuto alla proposta avanzata dal Fenerbahçe, l'allenatore attende una chiamata da uno dei cinque principali campionati europei, aprendo concretamente le porte a un ritorno in Serie A. Per il momento si tratta unicamente di una candidatura e non di una trattativa in corso, con il futuro di questa pista che dipenderà strettamente dalle prossime prove della squadra e dal test quasi decisivo di domenica contro la Lazio.

#2274. Calciomercato no stop - Indiscrezioni, trattative e retroscena del 5 novembre

INTER, LO SCHIAVO NUOVO DIRECTOR OF FOOTBALL DEVELOPMENT. ROMA, NEL MIRINO UN ESTERNO: PIACCIONO GITTENS E KVERNADZE. IL MILAN PAGHERÀ 3,5 MILIONI DI EURO PER AVERE KROSCHE. JUVENTUS, PER IL FUTURO OCCHI SU KOVACS. NAPOLI, MCTOMINAY CHIEDE TROPPO: RINNOVO DIFFICILE. IL TORINO SEGUE MIKOLAJEWSKI DEL PARMA.

La notizia era già nell'aria, ma adesso è arrivata anche l'ufficialità: Simone Lo Schiavo è il nuovo Director of Football Development dell'Inter. A renderlo noto è lo stesso club nerazzurro attraverso un comunicato sul proprio sito, nel quale lo ha accolto.

Il mercato di gennaio sarà fondamentale per la Roma. La priorità individuata dalla dirigenza è acquistare un esterno offensivo in grado di garantire imprevedibilità. Tra i nomi seguiti figurano Jamie Gittens del Chelsea, Endrick del Real Madrid, Jamie Leweling dello Stoccarda e Giorgi Kvernadze del Frosinone. In particolare, scrive oggi La Gazzetta dello Sport, quello del talento in forza ai Blues è un pro­filo che piace e che era stato già inse­guito nella sessione estiva.

Il Milan è pronto a pagare la clausola di risoluzione automatica per permettere a Markus Krosche di lasciare l'Eintracht Francoforte e diventare l'Head of Football dei rossoneri. Il Diavolo verserà nelle casse dei tedeschi 3,5 milioni di euro, il dirigente sarà operativo dall'1 gennaio 2027.

La Juventus ha sfidato l'AZ di Bendeguz Kovacs, attaccante del club olandese che i torinesi seguono con attenzione. Già la scorsa estate i bianconeri avevano provato a prenderlo a zero, ma il rinnovo fino al 2029 ha frenato la trattativa. A riportarlo è Tuttosport.

Scott McTominay chiedeva al Napoli 12 milioni di euro all'anno per rinnovare, poi però con il passare del tempo il centrocampista ha abbassato le proprie pretese, arrivando fino a 10. L'impressione è che a 8-9 si potrebbe chiudere l'accordo per il rinnovo, ma difficilmente De Laurentiis accetterà certe cifre e di conseguenza in estate la cessione resta un'ipotesi.

Il Torino continua a seguire l’attaccante polacco Daniel Mikolajewski, classe 2006 che sta facendo molto bene con la maglia del Lucerna, ma di proprietà del Parma. La valutazione in questi mesi è aumentata in maniera considerevole, si parla infatti non a caso di una cifra tra gli 8 e i 10 milioni di euro.

KANE RINNOVA COL BAYERN, RAPHINHA CON IL BARCELLONA. ALISSON VERSO L’ADDIO AL LIVERPOOL: CI SONO DUE CLUB BRASILIANO. EMERY-ASTON VILLA, PRONTO IL RINNOVO

Due rinnovi importanti per due top club europei: il Bayern Monaco ha infatti annunciato il rinnovo di Harry Kane che ha firmato fino al 30 giugno 2029. Il Pallone d’Oro 2026 ha scelto di proseguire la sua avventura in Baviera, dove dal suo arrivo nell’estate 2023 è diventato il simbolo del nuovo corso del club tedesco. Il Barcellona blinda uno dei protagonisti della sua nuova era. Il club catalano ha annunciato il rinnovo di contratto di Raphael Dias Belloli, meglio conosciuto come Raphinha, che ha prolungato il proprio accordo fino al 30 giugno 2030. Una scelta che certifica il peso raggiunto dal brasiliano all'interno del progetto blaugrana, dopo un percorso iniziato nell'estate del 2022.

Il futuro di Alisson Becker sarà lontano dal Liverpool con cui andrà a scadenza di contratto al termine della stagione. Sul portiere brasiliano potrebbe esserci nuovamente l’interesse della Juventus, che l’aveva seguito in estate, anche se al momento il Brasile sembra essere un’opzione più concreta. Il giocatore piace infatti molto al Flamengo, nonostante l’età non più giovanissima del portiere e l’ingaggio elevato, e all’Internacional Porto Alegre, club nel quale Alisson è cresciuto e a cui è rimasto fortemente legato, avendo in passato già espresso il desiderio di farvi ritorno.

Unai Emery è vicino a blindare il suo futuro all'Aston Villa e a spegnere definitivamente le voci su un possibile interesse del Manchester United. Il tecnico spagnolo, protagonista della rinascita del club negli ultimi quattro anni, è pronto a firmare un nuovo accordo (quello attuale scade nel 2029) con i Villans dopo i colloqui avanzati avuti mercoledì con il presidente Nassef Sawiris.

#2273. Verdict split in Watford vs Middlesbrough predictions as hosts show much-needed ‘grit’

Watford have endured a tough start to the Championship season under head coach Alessio Dionisi. They are positioned in 20th place in the table after 14 fixtures and are four points above the bottom three.

The Hornets won 1-0 at home against Lincoln City last time out which was a much needed victory. Summer signing Stephy Mavididi got their winner against the Imps in Hertfordshire.

Middlesbrough lost 2-0 away at Wrexham on Wednesday night and were without key striker Will Lankshear in North Wales due to injury. Boro missed his firepower in front of goal and slumped to a disappointing defeat at the Racecourse Ground.

Nevertheless, Boro remain top of the division as they eye promotion to the Premier League under manager Kim Hellberg in this campaign. They are one point above Wolves in 2nd and two ahead of Birmingham City, Stoke City and Swansea City.

“Watford’s win over Lincoln City last time out and eased some pressure off head coach Alessio Dionisi. They have now won their last two home games which should give them confidence ahead of this weekend.

“Despite their struggles, the Hornets do have some good individual players at their disposal like Stephy Mavididi, Iker Bravo and Othmane Maamma. They can cause problems for any opponents on their day when they are at it.

“Middlesbrough will be desperate to bounce back from their loss at Wrexham. They are a different team without Will Lankshear and if he is unavailable again on Saturday, they could be in trouble.

“Nevertheless, Boro are top for a reason and their defeat in Wales was their first one in the league since August in a run of spanning back 11 games. I think they may have to settle for a point in Hertfordshire though.”

“Watford have found a couple of important results in their last two home games. They haven’t been pretty but they’ve shown grit and got points on the board, which will matter in a battle against relegation to the Championship.

“This is a big task though, and Middlesbrough will be out for vengeance. Boro were really poor at Wrexham in the week, as they were far less efficient with their 62.2% possession than we have seen this campaign to date.

“When Kim Hellberg’s side last lost, they responded with a comprehensive 3-1 win over West Brom and went on an 11-game unbeaten run. They’ll be eager to bounce back in similar fashion, and I think they’ll be able to despite the Hornets’ improvements at home.

“I’m going for a comfortable away win as the visitors produce a strong response to their Wrexham disappointment.”

#2271. 'Maybe I will cry': Oh reflects on 'hard time' at Celtic before emotional return

Oh Hyeon-gyu departed Celtic for Genk in 2024 after falling out of favour under Brendan Rodgers.

Following 18 goal-laden months in Belgium, the South Korean striker joined Besiktas for €14 million at the start of this year.

In 34 appearances for the Turkish side, Oh has scored 12 goals.

It's a tally he wants to increase against his former employers at Celtic Park in the Europa League on Thursday evening.

"First of all, it's very special for me to be back here," Oh said. "I couldn't speak English properly back then. But everyone welcomed me like family here. And Celtic became like my second home.

"Maybe I will be emotional tomorrow. I'd be happy to score tomorrow. I'm Besiktas now. I will give everything for Besiktas."

Oh joined Celtic in January 2023 from Suwon Bluewings in his homeland.

"What can I say? I came here as a young boy with a dream," he continued. "It was sometimes hard for me, sometimes good for me.

"I have got good memories here. I will always be grateful for everything and the fans.

Now 25, Oh has progressed both on and off the pitch since swapping South Korea for Scotland almost four years ago.

"I learned a lot about my position from Kyogo Furuhashi and Adam Idah," Oh added. "It was a hard time for me because I didn't play a lot, sometimes I played, sometimes I did not.

"In my last season here, I didn't play for the last six months so it was a hard time for me. But I'm very happy to be back here because I love Celtic so much. It has a special place in my heart."

Ahead of Thursday's match, Besiktas boss Vincenzo Italiano said: "We have got points already but this is another important game.

"We are a team that’s very hungry. There may be changes to our team but we will give everything to get a positive result.

"We have been going well in our European games, winning our last game 3-0.  We have a different attention span for these games and have done a good job. It has not been easy but we have worked really hard. We are focused on qualifying on this phase and this game will be very tough.

"It’s a an amazing stadium and we are going to play against a very strong team with nice supporters. Celtic will want to win because they haven’t collected points yet.

"We have to be as hungry as them because it’s going to be a very difficult game. I played against Celtic at Bologna and it was a fantastic night. But this will be different.

"I am very much surprised they have zero points so we may see an angry Celtic team. This may complicate our job. It will be an important test for us. Everybody has to be really prepared."

#2270. L'incredibile controsenso post derby di Milan e Inter. Amorim e la doppia faccia insospettabile: bene in Serie A, disastro in Europa. La terza risposta della Juve su Spalletti: Rennes, Napoli e AZ, la squadra è con lui. Anche grazie a Carnevali

Il disastro del Milan, quello sfiorato dall'Atalanta e l'ennesimo sorriso europeo della Juventus. Questo, in estrema sintesi, il bilancio del giovedì di coppe per le italiane: due allenatori su tre non possono certo sorridere, Amorim e Sarri, mentre Spalletti, pur con troppa sofferenza nel finale, può farlo eccome, ma ci torneremo più avanti. Per prima cosa, infatti, il tema che vogliamo affrontare, tornando anche indietro di due giorni, è quello che riguarda l'incredibile e senza senso ritorno in campo di Inter e Milan dopo il derby di sabato scorso. A San Siro avevamo assistito a una partita che ci aveva lasciato un messaggio chiaro e preciso: rossoneri in netta crescita e nerazzurri sulle gambe dopo le tante gare giocate partendo da situazione di svantaggio che avevano lasciato il segno. Il calcio, però, e lo sappiamo molto bene, è strano, stranissimo. Tutto può cambiare in un attimo e spesso non accade ciò che sembra essere quasi scontato. Detto fatto. L'Inter di Chivu ha spazzato via il Feyenoord in trasferta, andando a mille all'ora e ripartendo dopo la prima batosta presa in campionato, con tanto di ennesimo derby perso, mentre il Milan si è consegnato al Ferencvaros a San Siro, uscendo tra i fischi di uno stadio che soltanto cinque giorni prima aveva dato vita a una vera e propria festa.

Un controsenso, appunto, perché il derby, almeno sulla carta, dovrebbe dare una spinta clamorosa a chi lo vince e mettere dubbi in testa a chi, invece, esce sconfitto, la le gare di Inter prima e Milan poi in Europa ci hanno detto l'esatto opposto. Poi un altro aspetto, incredibile anche questo. Ruben Amorim, tecnico portoghese arrivato in Serie A l'estate scorsa, sulla panchina rossonera, sta facendo un percorso completamente diverso tra campionato ed Europa League. Fin qui, in un certo senso, sarebbe anche una cosa che ci potevamo aspettare, però al contrario. Spieghiamoci meglio: un allenatore, spesso, nei primi mesi in Italia, arrivando dall'estero, può andare incontro ad alcuni problemi, prevedibili, viste le particolari caratteristiche del nostro calcio, per trovarsi più a suo agio nelle competizioni internazionali, ma ad Amorim sta succedendo l'opposto. Bene in Serie A, visti i 21 punti raccolti nelle prime dieci giornate, solo quattro in meno dell'Inter campione d'Italia, e male in Europa League, dove di fatto ha già compromesso, con la sua squadra, ma qualificazione diretta agli ottavi di finale, avendo solo tre punti dopo le prime quattro gare. Strano, molto strano.

Infine un capitolo a parte sulla Juventus, dopo la terza vittoria in Europa. L'ultimo periodo è stato complicatissimo, lo sappiamo e lo abbiamo detto in tutte le salse, ma ci sono state tre partite delle ultime quattro che ci hanno detto una cosa inequivocabile: lo spogliatoio è con Luciano Spalletti. I tre indizi che fanno una prova sono le gare contro Rennes, Napoli e AZ, lasciando da parte quelle contro Lecce e Genoa. Nel momento di massima pressione i giocatori hanno lottato con il coltello tra i denti, dimostrando di essere dalla parte del tecnico e adesso il vento sembra davvero essere cambiato. Le parole di Giovanni Carnevali dopo il ko a Marassi hanno fatto il resto. Il dirigente ha parlato alla squadra, ha confermato l'allenatore e ha alzato i toni chiedendo responsabilità a chi va in campo gara dopo gara. Un discorso duro, che ha pagato e sta pagando. Adesso la Juve sfiderà la Fiorentina, poi ci sarà la sosta. Con tre punti al Franchi la crisi sarebbe solo un lontano ricordo. Sia chiaro.

#2269. Dave Challinor confirms Championship job desire and rival interest before Sheffield Wednesday swoop

Sheffield Wednesday officially announced Dave Challinor as their new boss earlier this week. The move marked the 51-year-old’s first job since leaving Stockport County in the summer, but his success with the Hatters meant SWFC weren’t alone in eyeing him.

Speaking to The Star, Challinor revealed other clubs wanted ‘conversations’, and his eyes were initially on a step up to the Championship before joining the Owls – a club with ambitions of returning to the second tier. He said: “Without going into too much detail, obviously people wanted to have conversations. I was in the fortunate position where I didn’t need to jump back in. I came out of a really good football club, and we had success, so I had the luxury of being able to be a little bit selective and wait.

“My natural progression would have been to try and get a Championship opportunity. That made the number of clubs I am appealing to was probably pretty small. The next opportunity either needed to give me that chance, or be one I could grow with. I certainly couldn’t have picked a bigger one in terms of this club and the amount of growth it’s got in it.”

Challinor was more than justified in initially eyeing a step up to the Championship after his Stockport County departure. He took the Hatters from the National League to the brink of promotion from League One, but there was a mutual agreement that it was a good time to part ways.

His back-to-back top six finishes in League One and promotion pedigree made him the ideal candidate for Sheffield Wednesday. And, if the Chester-born boss wasn’t going to land a Championship job, moving to a third-tier side with promotion ambitions made for the next best option.

Arguably though, the SWFC job is more exciting than many in the Championship. The ongoing rebuild under new ownership and desire to wake this sleeping giant makes the managerial post a highly attractive one, so Challinor is progressing in job stature despite staying in the same league.

Challinor is a coup for the Owls, and it’s an exciting step for him too. It’s a win-win appointment that benefits all parties, as long as results follow.

Challinor watched on from the stands as Sheffield Wednesday played out a 1-1 draw with Blackpool under Matt Hamshaw last week. The new boss has been able to get to work with his squad this week though, and the first game is just around the corner.

SWFC face League Two side Oldham Athletic in the FA Cup on Sunday. It marks a favourable start for Challinor, both in terms of the level of the opposition and the fact that there aren’t points at stake.

David Storch has been eager to stress that this appointment is part of a long-term rebuild, and success will not be defined by how this season goes. Nevertheless, Challinor will be determined to get Wednesday firing as soon as he can to strengthen their case for a spot in League One’s top six at the very least.

#2266. Hearts reveal investment in push for European return resulted in £2.4m loss

Hearts have announced a loss of £2.4million after investing heavily to return to European football this season.

The Tynecastle club backed Derek McInnes after he took over as boss in the summer of 2025.

The Jambos’ thrilling William Hill Premiership title challenge went to the last day of the season when they lost to Celtic at Parkhead to finish second to the Hoops.

Hearts lost to Sturm Graz in the Champions League qualifiers and then to Benfica in the Europa League, but victory over Rapid Vienna saw Wouter Vrancken’s side make it to the league phase of the Conference League, where they face Swiss side Thun on Thursday.

Hearts also reported a turnover of £23.1m for the 2025/26 financial year, are also in “positive” discussions about further investment to help Vrancken in the January transfer window and are confident of a “significantly improved financial result” in a year’s time.

Derek McInnes on Findlay Curtis Rangers future as he reveals player stance The Celtic youngster Martin O'Neill was keen to check on during Ross County clash

The club’s financial results also revealed that staff costs went up eight per cent “with player acquisition costs of £7.8m across the year, reflecting significant investment in the playing squad”.

A statement on Hearts’ official website said: “The club took a deliberate decision to invest in the playing squad from a position of financial strength, helping secure a return to European league phase football for 2026/27.

“This additional investment in squad wages, combined with the absence of European income, resulted in an operating loss of £2.4m for the year.

“With European income returning and further progress in player trading, the club anticipates a significantly improved financial result in 2026/27.

“Strong underlying revenue growth offset much of the loss of European income for the 2025/26 financial year, with sponsorship and advertising income up 37 per cent and retail and hospitality income up 17 per cent, reflecting continued commercial progress and support from partners and fans alike.

“The club continued to benefit from exceptional financial backing from its benefactors and Foundation of Hearts members, and extends its sincere thanks for their continued commitment and support.

“The increased investment in the playing squad and football operations was supported by an investment from Tony Bloom towards the end of the previous financial year, which strengthened the club’s financial position and provided capacity to invest in improving the playing squad.

“As an equity investment, this funding is reflected in the balance sheet rather than recognised as income in the profit and loss account.

“The reported operating loss of £2.4m includes £1.8m of gains from player trading and is stated before depreciation, amortisation and finance costs.

“Further gains will be reflected in the 2026/27 financial year following player trading activity completed in July and August 2026.

“The club is also engaged in positive discussions regarding further investment to support the continued, measured pursuit of the club’s ambitions.

“The club’s balance sheet remains robust, with the accounts reporting net assets of £20.3m at the June 30 2026 financial year-end.”

#2264. Exclusive: Matt Jarvis backs ‘exceptional’ Scott Parker in verdict on West Ham’s Nuno sacking

West Ham parted ways with Nuno Espirito Santo on Tuesday, and swiftly named Scott Parker as his replacement. The new boss led WHUFC for Wednesday’s 3-3 draw at Cardiff City, and many are backing him for success.

Among the supporters of Parker is former Hammers winger Matt Jarvis, who believes inconsistency ultimately cost Nuno his job. Speaking to The72 via Midnite, Jarvis said: “The fans’ perspective flipped from last year’s misery to expecting to dominate this league and win promotion. Visiting the London Stadium in front of 55,000 to 60,000 supporters is every opposition team’s cup final, and adapting to that takes time.

“With Nuno, people forget he had been there for a significant period. It took him time to establish himself in the Premier League, then they had a good spell before fading towards the end. The board backed him in the transfer market after relegation, but results remained too inconsistent. The supporters couldn’t see a clear playing identity, and the home defeat to West Brom proved the final straw.”

Jarvis went on to talk up Parker’s chances of success as West Ham boss. He picked out his communication skills, experience and passion as key traits as he looks to take the Hammers back to the Premier League.

“People will debate whether the decision came too early or whether they should have waited for the international break, but the club felt a change was needed. They have four upcoming fixtures where they should realistically target three points from every game.

“Scott Parker has a fantastic Championship track record and an existing connection to the club.

“He’s an exceptional communicator, which is vital for translating training-ground instruction onto the pitch on matchdays. He’s passionate, knows what it takes to get out of this division, and his arrival should have an immediate impact.”

Parker has won three promotions from the Championship, achieving the feat at Fulham, Bournemouth and Burnley. In his most recent triumph, he led the Clarets to 2nd place in 2024/25 and broke the league record for the fewest goals conceded in an entire campaign at just 16.

The switch from Nuno to Parker has split some opinions. There are justified concerns that their play styles are too similar given the new boss’ recent teams have been built on the foundation of a strong defence.

This was the case in his last job at Burnley, but the 46-year-old has shown he has the ability to switch up his approach. At Fulham in the 2020/21 season, his side had the second-highest average possession in the Championship with 61.4% (FotMob), as well 85 big chances created and 1,101 touches in the opposition box.

Over time, it will become clear whether Parker plans on emulating his Burnley tactics at WHUFC, or his Fulham style. Alternatively, he could switch things up completely, and if he does, ex-Hammer Jarvis believes he has the know-how and communication skills to get this across to his new squad.

After taking charge of the Cardiff City draw a matter of hours after his appointment on Wednesday, Parker leads his side for the second time against Blackburn Rovers this Saturday.

Midnite, the Principal Partner of Wolverhampton Wanderers for the 2026/27 season, are providing luxury coach travel on the “Midnite Express” for 100 Wolverhampton Wanderers supporters, as they set off on a 130-mile-round trip to watch their beloved Wolves take on the league’s glamour club, Wrexham, this Saturday, November 7.

Fans will be joined on the Express by Wolves club legends, Steve Bull, Carl Ikeme and Matt Jarvis and treated to club-centric quizzes and live Q&As, with exclusive prizes up for grabs.

La FIGC ha reso noti i convocati scelti da Roberto Mancini per il doppio impegno di Nations League.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Certificata la ripartenza dopo la mancata qualificazione al Mondiale, la Nazionale tornerà a radunarsi domenica sera a Coverciano in vista delle ultime due gare del girone di Nations League. Il doppio successo ottenuto nella lunga finestra di settembre/ottobre con la Turchia e il pareggio in rimonta nel match di Saint-Denis con la Francia hanno permesso agli Azzurri di portarsi al secondo posto in classifica nel Gruppo 1 della Lega A a quota sette punti, tre in meno della capolista Francia e uno in più del Belgio: e proprio i Bleus e i Diavoli Rossi saranno gli avversari dell’Italia nelle sfide in programma giovedì 12 novembre allo stadio Giuseppe Meazza di Milano (emessi 57.000 biglietti, in vendita gli ultimi 3.000 tagliandi) e domenica 15 novembre allo stadio Re Baldovino di Bruxelles.

Già sicura almeno del terzo posto e di essere quindi tra le teste di serie al sorteggio per le qualificazioni europee in programma il prossimo 6 dicembre a Belfast, l’Italia va a caccia di altri punti per ipotecare la permanenza nella Lega A (le due migliori terze classificate si salvano senza bisogno di ricorrere agli spareggi) e con l’ambizione di chiudere il girone nelle prime due posizioni conquistando così il pass per i quarti di finale.

Se per le prime quattro gare – racchiuse nell’arco di undici giorni – Roberto Mancini aveva puntato su un gruppo allargato di 34 calciatori, stavolta il Ct ne ha convocati 31. Quattro giovani sono alla prima chiamata in Nazionale maggiore: il centrocampista classe 2009 del Como Riccardo Cassano, il centrocampista classe 2008 del Parma Simone Lontani, l’attaccante classe 2006 del Milan Alphadjo Cisse e l’attaccante classe 2007 del Como Mattia Liberali.

Per quanto riguarda Cassano e Lontani, Mancini ha voluto convocarli per poterli osservare da vicino e per dar loro una preziosa opportunità per mettersi in mostra: non resteranno aggregati al gruppo per tutta la durata del raduno, ma nei prossimi giorni raggiungeranno le Nazionali Under 18 e Under 19, impegnate rispettivamente nel Mondiale al via il 19 novembre in Qatar e nella seconda fase delle qualificazioni al Campionato Europeo in programma dall’11 al 17 novembre nelle Marche.

Portieri: Marco Carnesecchi (Atalanta), Gianluigi Donnarumma (Manchester City), Guglielmo Vicario (Juventus);

Difensori: Honest Ahanor (Crystal Palace), Alessandro Bastoni (Inter), Riccardo Calafiori (Arsenal), Federico Dimarco (Inter), Daniele Ghilardi (Roma), Michael Kayode (Brentford), Gianluca Mancini (Roma), Marco Palestra (Chelsea), Giorgio Scalvini (Atalanta);

Centrocampisti: Nicolò Barella (Inter), Riccardo Cassano (Como), Issa Doumbia (Sporting CP), Nicolò Fagioli (Fiorentina), Davide Frattesi (Lazio), Simone Lontani (Parma), Niccolò Pisilli (Roma), Samuele Ricci (Como), Alessandro Romano (Cagliari), Sandro Tonali (Tottenham);

#2262. ‘I hold my hands up’ - McAvennie issues fresh apology to Celtic star Auston Trusty

Frank McAvennie has issued a full apology to Auston Trusty and his family after admitting his initial response to criticism over comments about the Celtic defender “didn’t go far enough”.

The former Celtic striker faced an online backlash after questioning the length of time Trusty had been away from football following the death of his father.

McAvennie has now returned to the issue and said he is “genuinely sorry” for any hurt caused by his remarks.

In a fresh apology, he said: “Frank here. I’ve had more time to think about what I said about Auston on the podcast and I know my statement yesterday didn’t go far enough.

“I’m genuinely sorry for any hurt I’ve caused Auston or his family. That’s the last thing I’d ever want.

“I hold my hands up and I’ve learned my lesson.

“I genuinely wish Auston and his family all the very best.”

McAvennie's original comments came on his Let Me Be Frank podcast as he discussed Trusty's absence from the Celtic team.

He said: “I’ve got every respect for that, but how long do you want off?

“Martin (O’Neill) says he’s away to celebrate the life of his dad.

Hearts reveal investment in push for European return resulted in £2.4m loss Derek McInnes on Findlay Curtis Rangers future as he reveals player stance

“Celebrate the life? You’ve got football. You’re getting paid and we’re in trouble. How long is it going to be?

“It’s nonsense. My dad was the best man, the only man I looked up to, when he died.

Following criticism of those remarks, McAvennie initially acknowledged that he should have chosen his words more carefully.

He said: “I can understand why people feel my comments were insensitive, and looking back, I should have chosen my words more carefully.

“Everyone deals with grief differently, and I certainly meant no disrespect to Auston or his family.

“Celtic need the big man back, and that’s what I was clumsily trying to say.

“I wish him and his family all the very best.”

McAvennie has now conceded that statement did not adequately address his original remarks and offered a more direct apology to the Celtic defender and his family.

#2258. Blackburn Rovers sweat over injuries to two key defenders as West Ham challenge awaits

Blackburn Rovers face West Ham in their final game before the November international break this Saturday. They come into the tie off the back of a 2-1 defeat against Stoke City, and Tony Mowbray’s side were without centre-back Tom Atcheson for the tie, while full-back Ryan Alebiosu came off in the first half.

Now, speaking to the press, Mowbray has said the Rovers duo will require further assessment to see if they can face the Hammers as they struggle with minor issues. As quoted by the club’s official media, the boss said: “Big Tom has still got a little niggle in his groin, so it’s about whether we take a chance with him or not. Ryan is going to go for a scan, and I hope it isn’t too bad.”

Alebiosu has been a mainstay for Blackburn Rovers this season, starting every Championship match and playing the full 90 minutes in all but two games. Atcheson meanwhile has made 11 appearances in all competitions, including 10 league starts.

Blackburn Rovers aren’t blessed for depth, so it would be a blow to go into Saturday’s game against West Ham without Alebiosu and Atcheson. Mowbray will have to work out a solution if either are ruled out.

Alebiosu has been the mainstay at right-back this season, but his absence would likely offer an opportunity for summer signing Gady Beyuku to impress. The 20-year-old has only made four substitute appearances to date, but he came on for 49 minutes against the Potters on Tuesday.

At centre-back, Hayden Carter partnered Sean McLoughlin with Atcheson out. The former has had injury problems of his own in recent times, so Mowbray may be hesitant to put him in for another full 90 minutes just a few days later.

This is an area where Mowbray has very little depth though. Scott Wharton was the only other central defender in the squad on Tuesday, and he has been on a long road back of his own.

It means Carter may start again if Atcheson is out, with Wharton called upon off the bench late on.

Of course, the hope will be that both Alebiosu and Atcheson are fit and ready to go against West Ham this weekend. However, if they aren’t deemed fit, the Blackburn Rovers defenders will have the international break to recover and rest up before the next club fixture.

After Saturday, the next league fixture sees Rovers head to Turf Moor to face rivals Burnley. Mowbray will be determined to have key men Alebiosu and Atcheson available for the fixture.

One potential worry is Atcheson’s call-up to the Northern Ireland squad for the upcoming international fixtures. If he isn’t deemed fit for Saturday, there will be questions over whether he should link up with Michael O’Neill‘s national side as planned.

Nigeria have not announced their squad for their AFCON qualifiers, but Alebiosu would be in a similar predicament if he is included in their ranks.

#2257. Hearts get bad news on Calvin Miller in latest injury blow

Hearts boss Wouter Vrancken revealed Calvin Miller will require surgery on a knee injury that will keep him out for two months.

Both Tomas Magnusson and Miller came off during the first half of the Jambos’ 2-1 Premier Sports Cup semi-final defeat to Edinburgh rivals Hibernian at Barclays Hampden on Sunday.

The Iceland midfielder was taken to hospital with concussion and the former Celtic, Morton and Falkirk attacker left the national stadium on crutches and his knee in a brace.

Vrancken, who will be without 10 first-team players for the Conference League game against FC Thun on Thursday in Switzerland, was asked about Miller and said: “It will be two months, let’s say, depending on what the surgery says because it’s a meniscus injury.

“Tomas is doing good. The day after was already a lot better.

“There is a protocol, of course, with concussions. He will be for sure back after the (international) break.”

Vrancken will have to put together a side capable of getting a first win in the competition, after a 1-1 home draw against Nordsjaelland and a 2-0 defeat away to Trabzonspor.

‘I hold my hands up’ - McAvennie issues fresh apology to Celtic star Auston Trusty Hearts reveal investment in push for European return resulted in £2.4m loss

He said: “Yes, it’s a puzzle. I’m always happy to see that the players we work with and play out of position or talk with are always directly motivated to do it.

“They know how much they help the team by doing it.

“It’s good to see because they take responsibility and they don’t live by excuses.

“They have already done it for weeks. That’s how we also get good results and good performances.

“Now we have to try to do it two more times before the break.”

Vrancken is confident his side will respond positively to their defeat by Hibs in Glasgow.

He said: “I think so. They showed already a few times this season that they can bounce back in performance.

“I think when you leave out the last 20-25 minutes of the first half, we played a good game.

“It’s just a pity that we couldn’t reward ourselves in the second half with a goal that could spin it around.

“But I’m confident in the players that they are mentally ready to play the game.

“They (Thun) have a good team, they have a lot of energy, they have a lot of intensity. They have direct play, going fast forward a lot of moments.

“With a lot of energy, good quality players also in front.

“We will have to be spot on, we have to be on our level to make the result. But the aim is to try to get a win here.”

#2254. Di’Shon Bernard fit but three players still out as Dave Challinor issues Sheffield Wednesday injury latest

Sheffield Wednesday face Oldham Athletic in the FA Cup on Sunday as Dave Challinor takes charge of his new side for the first time. The new SWFC boss has a squad full of talented players to pick from, but he has injuries to consider too.

And, speaking to the press ahead of his maiden game, Challinor confirmed that trio Liam Cooper, Joe Lumley and Ali Al-Hamadi are set to remain out. Cooper is more of a precautionary decision, while Lumley and Al-Hamadi are targeting returns to training next week.

In better news, Di’Shon Bernard is fit and available having trained all week. Challinor and the Owls will remain cautious with his load management after a long time on the sidelines.

Cup matches against opponents in lower divisions often offers teams the chance to rotate, giving fringe players the chance to come in and impress. It will be intriguing to see just what sort of approach Challinor takes though, as he gets to grips with his Sheffield Wednesday squad.

Early on, Challinor has to determine his strongest starting lineup. The most efficient way to do so will be by playing as strong a side as possible in this FA Cup clash with Oldham Athletic.

Alternatively, Challinor will have done his research before taking over at SWFC, and he watched the Blackpool draw. If he already has a good idea of his favoured XI, he can take this game to give chances to those projected to be on the fringes to get a clearer picture of the full squad at his disposal.

It is an interesting dilemma for Challinor, and it will be interesting to see how he approaches it.

Lumley’s continued absence means Ashley Maynard-Brewer will likely keep his place in goal. Cooper being on the sidelines gives Bernard a good chance of coming straight into the side as well.

Challinor had a strong squad at his disposal at Stockport County, but it feels like this Sheffield Wednesday group is a level up. There are experienced pros like Cooper, Barry Bannan and Liam Palmer, alongside players who could arguably be at a higher level, such as Callum Slattery, Billy Mitchell and defender Bernard.

Pair this with the youthful exuberance of Ernie Weaver, Will Grainger and Harry Gray, and SWFC have a squad built for the short and long-term. There’s a good balance of profiles and characters, making up a recipe for success at Hillsborough.

Challinor faces plenty of selection headaches in deciding his best lineup, and he will be excited to find it out.
