#2253. Liverpool lead twice before losing to Fenerbahce in Champions League

Even Liverpool’s multi-million pound bench could not rescue a team floundering in Istanbul as a chaotic performance was punished by Fenerbahce as they twice came from behind to win 3-2.

Alexander Isak had scored within three minutes of coming on to put the visitors ahead going into the final quarter but, like in the first half, the lead lasted just four minutes before former West Brom trainee Archie Brown equalised courtesy of some lax defending.

But worse was to follow as Vedat Muriqi outjumped three players to nod in the winner and end Liverpool’s 100 per cent record.

And while Andoni Iraola will be scrutinised for making five changes, including resting three of his front four in Sunday’s defeat to Arsenal, it was the way the team lost their cool and control in a game they had in hand which was more damaging.

It was a harsh result on 19-year-old Trey Nyoni, who belied his relative inexperience with a composed performance capped by an excellent volley to open the scoring, becoming Liverpool’s youngest player to register his maiden goal in the Champions League and second-youngest scorer in the competition after Trent Alexander-Arnold.

But Liverpool’s Turkish troubles continued – they lost twice to Galatasaray last season – on a typically fractious night in Istanbul, with former Manchester United forward Mason Greenwood scoring the first equaliser with a free-kick which deflected in off Jeremy Jacquet.

They had been in total command up to that point but from there the game descended into something more manic, which suited a team hammered 6-0 at Villa Park last month and stoked a febrile home crowd.

Hearts get bad news on Calvin Miller in latest injury blow ‘I hold my hands up’ - McAvennie issues fresh apology to Celtic star Auston Trusty

Many of Liverpool’s players did not cope well in that atmosphere but, to his immense credit, Nyoni shone.

Iraola’s choice to leave out Isak, Florian Wirtz and Cody Gakpo as they come to the end of a run of nine games in 27 days was understandable but it left his side toothless.

Bradley Barcola was deployed as striker but his unfamiliarity with the role was evident after failing to properly anticipate Joe Gomez’s low cross to the near post.

He did flick one header wide but struggled with the physicality of Fenerbahce’s centre-backs, even though Kojo Oppong was making his first Champions League start.

There was a lot of naivety within the visitors’ ranks – Victor Munoz snatched at an early chance when he was found in acres of space before redeeming himself by eventually forcing Ederson into a save – but none of it came from Nyoni.

The teenager was composed on just his seventh Champions League appearance – with everything going through him at the base of midfield.

His use of the ball, tactical awareness and unshakeable belief in his own ability meant amid the hurly burly of a typically Turkish clash he was head and shoulders above everyone else.

Even the usually unflappable Virgil van Dijk’s patience was tested, pushing Irfan Kahveci to the floor at a second-half corner, having twice had Romelu Lukaku leave something on him in the early stages.

When Liverpool’s breakthrough came it was fully deserved as Rio Ngumoha’s cross to the far post saw Van Dijk’s pressure force Brown to head weakly to the edge of the penalty area where Nyoni dispatched the dropping ball via a deflection off Oppong.

But after a sliding Kahveci smashed against the crossbar from five yards, Greenwood’s free-kick went through the wall.

Isak headed in Gakpo’s cross with his first chance after those two and Wirtz were summoned in the 66th minute, but the unmarked Brown tapped in the equaliser and more poor defending saw Muriqi head home the winner.

#2252. Serie B, Ascoli-Palermo: i marchigiani per il colpaccio, i siciliani per la fuga in vetta

Si accendono i riflettori sullo stadio “Cino e Lillo Del Duca” di Ascoli, teatro dell’attesissimo anticipo dell’11ª giornata del campionato di Serie B tra Ascoli e Palermo. Va in scena un vero e proprio scontro di alta classifica tra due formazioni che stanno dominando la scena in questo avvio di stagione. I padroni di casa, autentica sorpresa del torneo, sognano a occhi aperti la Serie A, occupando il terzo posto con 19 punti in 10 giornate (a una sola lunghezza dal Modena secondo e a 7 dalla capolista Palermo). Il pareggio di Arezzo ha lasciato un pizzico di rammarico, ma la voglia di misurarsi contro la capolista e strappare i tre punti è immensa. Dall'altra parte, il Palermo guida la classifica con autorità ma deve fare i conti con un'infermeria affollata, specie nel reparto arretrato. I rosanero, tuttavia, non cercano alibi e sono pronti a dare battaglia con l'undici tipo.

COME ARRIVA L’ASCOLI - L’Ascoli si presenta a questo appuntamento cullato dal calore straordinario della propria piazza, che ha mandato sold-out il settore dello stadio Del Duca: saranno quasi 15.000 i tifosi bianconeri pronti a spingere il "Picchio" in una delle sfide più importanti degli ultimi anni. I ragazzi di Tomei hanno preparato la gara nei minimi dettagli, consapevoli dell’opportunità di consolidarsi nei piani altissimi della classifica.

Tomei deve fare a meno dell’indisponibile Alagna, ancora ai box per la riabilitazione, mentre resta in dubbio l'ex Damiani per noie fisiche. Per il resto, al netto di sorprese dell'ultima ora, l'undici titolare dovrebbe rispecchiare quello visto nelle ultime uscite. Davanti a Vitale, la linea difensiva a quattro dovrebbe comporsi con Oliveri (o alternative di reparto), Curado, Rizzo e Guiebre. In mediana agiranno Corradini e Milanese, mentre sulla trequarti i riflettori sono tutti puntati sull'ex Silipo, protagonista di un avvio di stagione scintillante, affiancato da Bolsius e dal creativo D’Uffizi. In avanti ballottaggio aperto per il ruolo di centravanti, con Gori favorito per guidare l'attacco bianconero contro la corazzata rosanero.

COME ARRIVA IL PALERMO - Il Palermo arriva a questa sfida con la consapevolezza di vantare il miglior attacco del torneo, ma si appresta a misurarsi con la miglior difesa della Serie B (appena 6 reti subite dai marchigiani). I rosanero guidano la classifica con 8 vittorie e 2 pareggi in 10 giornate, entrambi ottenuti lontano dal Barbera. Inzaghi ha intenzione di evitare esperimenti tattici azzardati, affidandosi all’ossatura più collaudata possibile.

Una scelta dettata anche dalla coperta corta in un'infermeria che scotta: le ultime notizie dall'elenco degli indisponibili sono pesanti e riguardano i gravi stop di Bani (lesione muscolare) e Pierozzi (lussazione alla spalla con intervento chirurgico in vista), i quali rischiano di aver chiuso anzitempo il loro 2026. A loro si aggiungono Joronen, Strefezza, Palumbo e (con ogni probabilità) Barba.

Nonostante le defezioni, il tecnico schiererà il consueto 4-2-3-1: tra i pali ci sarà Perin; in difesa Cassandro sulla destra, i confermati Ceccaroni e Augello, con Peda in netto vantaggio su Magnani per completare il pacchetto centrale. A centrocampo possibili novità per gestire le energie prima della sosta: Estevez scalpita per concedere un turno di riposo a Ranocchia, mentre Segre è in vantaggio su Mazzitelli per l'altra maglia in mediana. Sulla trequarti intoccabili Vavassori e Johnsen, supportati da Hernani nel suo raggio d'azione ibrido tra mezzala e trequartista. In avanti, il terminale offensivo sarà Pohjanpalo, a caccia del ritorno al gol dopo il digiuno dalle gare esterne.

#2251. Derby County dealt another injury blow with key forward set for six weeks out

Derby County went another game without a win as they were torn apart by Norwich City 4-0 on Tuesday night. John Eustace is under serious pressure as DCFC host Bolton Wanderers, in what could be his last chance to turn things round at Pride Park.

The Rams have not been helped by injuries this season and they have been dealt yet another blow now. John Eustace has told the BBC that Sammie Szmodics is set for surgery on a knee injury that will see him out for six weeks.

The forward limped off in the defeat to Norwich City during the second-half. Szmodics had been playing through the injury but after aggravating it on Tuesday, surgery was the only option.

He has made a decent start to this season having returned to the club in the summer after an initial loan deal last term.

Losing Szmodics a significant blow for Derby County, particularly given their precarious position in the Championship. Eustace’s side sit 23rd with just seven points from 14 games, having recorded only one win all season.

Szmodics is joint-top scorer with three league goals, alongside Bobby Clark, and has also provided one assist. Eustace described the loss as “huge” after confirming Szmodics will undergo surgery and miss approximately six weeks, potentially ruling him out until mid-December.

DCFC already have several players unavailable. While six weeks might ordinarily be manageable, the Rams’ lack of goals, creativity and squad depth makes this a considerable setback as they attempt to escape the relegation zone.

Filip Bilbija looks the most likely direct replacement for Szmodics in the No.10 role, with Bobby Clark another strong option. Szmodics has predominantly featured as an attacking midfielder in Eustace’s preferred 4-2-3-1 formation, operating behind the striker with freedom to get forward.

Bilbija has already been tipped to fill that position against Bolton, allowing Clark to remain in the double pivot alongside Lewis Travis or Alex Mowatt. Clark is also joint-top scorer with three goals and has the versatility to play further forward if required.

Other alternatives include Rhian Brewster, who can operate centrally or out wide, while Mohammed Fuseini and Corey Blackett-Taylor offer further attacking options. Henrik Meister and Carlton Morris could also provide cover in a supporting role.

Ryan Hedges is another fitness doubt, adding to Derby’s injury concerns. Eustace has options, but none quite replicate Szmodics’ experience and goal threat in the No.10 position.

#2250. Celtic boss Martin O'Neill responds to Frank McAvennie's Auston Trusty remarks

Celtic manager Martin O'Neill has criticised comments made by former Parkhead forward Frank McAvennie about Auston Trusty following the sad passing of his father.

McAvennie questioned the amount of time the American had taken off to celebrate the life of his dad, before apologising via a statement on X.

Speaking ahead of Celtic's Europa League clash with Besiktas, O'Neill responded to McAvennie's initial remarks.

The 74-year-old explained that Trusty had requested leave and that he is with the defender 'the whole way'.

"That's really poor taste," O'Neill said of McAvennie's comments. "I know I've said some things and ended up backtracking. But that's a family bereavement.

"And that's making someone's mind up about how long they should be grieving and stuff like this here. That's not on.

"Trusty asked me for some time [off]. Even if it's a celebration [of his late father’s life], it doesn't matter. The father's been in poor health for quite some considerable time.

"And I know there was a stage where he thought that he wasn't going to see it through. But I'm with Trusty the whole way there. Because I wouldn't want anybody to tell me that I had to grieve over a brother or sister dying. And I've had that.

"He's 28 years of age. And he's taken it badly. Honestly. I've afforded him all the time he wants.

"At some stage, obviously, he'll be coming back. But at the end of the day, I've given that time because I know that we might be talking about different eras or whatever the case may be. That's nothing to do with it. This is a family thing. And that's not right.

"So, Trusty has got the time and he's got the backing of myself and he's got the backing of the club."

#2249. Celtic AGM latest as O'Neill talks long-term future and tense CSC visit

For Martin O'Neill, it has already been a hectic week. On Monday evening, he attended a meeting at Greenock Celtic Supporters' Club alongside interim chairman Brian Wilson, chief executive Michael Nicholson, and chief financial officer Chris McKay. Over 120 fans were present as the Celtic manager and board faced, according to the club, 'hard but fair' questioning.

Less than 24 hours later, O'Neill found himself in Dingwall with assistant coaches Mark Fotheringham and Stephen McManus watching Celtic B lose 2-0 to Ross County in the Scottish Challenge Cup.

That's all before two crucial home fixtures against Besiktas in the Europa League and Kilmarnock in the William Hill Premiership come into the fray.

The 74-year-old sat down ahead of the tie with Besiktas to discuss Monday's meeting, his long-term future, and the club's upcoming Annual General Meeting (AGM).

"First of all, I wasn't really aware of the meeting, so it was kind of an ad hoc thing," O'Neill explained. "But I obviously was around. I spoke to Michael [Nicholson] and he said that they were going to meet.

"And I said, well, I wouldn't mind going down to it if that was the case, obviously to experience what the fans would be asking.

"The meeting itself was, I think you could use the word robust, yes, firm, but very orderly questioning.

"And trying to give the answers that, not necessarily what people are looking for, because I'm not so sure that will ever reach a satisfactory stage for a while anyway. But, yeah, it was really interesting and an experience.

"I probably didn't really need to go to a meeting, but these people were big Celtic fans. And naturally, I think both board and supporters, I thought there was a wanting the best for the football club.

"At the end of the day, there are bad times and I think that you should face up to that. Was everything answered by the board and myself to their satisfaction? One, you'd have to ask the people there at the time what was there.

"There was no shouting. It wasn't like the AGM that appeared [last year]. The shouting had taken place before the meeting."

Indeed, as O'Neill and company arrived in Greenock, a number of Celtic supporters wearing balaclavas were outside the supporters' club, voicing their own concerns.

"To me, I think that was the saddest thing," O'Neill continued. "Would I have ever, in any day, ever thought that Celtic fans would be clothed in balaclavas before a meeting? Even if they were disapproving of what was happening. I think that's really disappointing.

"I do find it hard to get my head round that kind of stuff.

"I think if you've got a dispute or something, I think you should be brave enough to show your face doing it.

"[It was] just on the way in. I don't know whether they expected me to be there or not, but it doesn't really matter. It was just regardless of who was there."

The hope is that this year's AGM doesn't entail similar hostilities. The last iteration was adjourned early following animosity between the hierarchy and supporters.

It's understood the 2026 AGM will take place in early December in person.

"I would expect to be at it as a manager," O'Neill said. "So if given an opportunity to answer some of the things and if they want to hear from the manager, I would have no problem.

"I hope it's similar to what Monday night was in terms of the questions and fairness and not falling into what it was last year."

As O'Neill discusses myriad off-pitch matters, it's easy to forget he's Celtic's football manager.

"People are asking me now if it’s something I’m going to continue to do," he added. "No, my main job depends on results and winning some football matches. That’s the most important thing."

Contracted at Celtic Park until the end of this season, O'Neill's deal has the option of a further 12 months.

This time last year, he was two games into what was meant to be the most fleeting of interim spells.

Wilfried Nancy's disastrous reign came and went and O'Neill returned in January, winning the double and staying on as permanent manager in the summer.

When asked if this was his final season in charge, O'Neill responded: "I can’t imagine anybody asking me back. Seriously, I can’t imagine that happening.

"Listen, I didn’t come in here today to give a final word at this minute."

If the coming months are to be his last, how important is it to go out on a high?

"It would be nice to see the club that I knew intimately from 2000 – having followed from a distance since my school days – be successful.

"Did I ever think I’d see a day when 75,000 people would descend on a UEFA Cup final day, with no arrests? There are still pictures on the wall here and all you see is a sea of green and white. To see that again, that would be your dream."

Celtic, though, could not be further away from reaching another European final. Sunday's defeat to Falkirk was their sixth in 10 matches, with Besiktas up next in the Europa League.

From their first three fixtures in the competition, Celtic are pointless, heightening criticism on O'Neill and his coaches.

"Everyone is a tactician now, that’s the whole point," he said. "Everybody is a football manager. There are people who have got jobs in football now – at football clubs – because they played PlayStation Manager.

"Honestly, it’s extraordinary. But it does not bother me, honestly, it doesn’t. People talk about tactics but in my semi-defence, no-one talked about tactics when we won the seven games to win the league [last season]. Or the 14 games we won before we got beat.

"And you know what, you’re right to think – if you got beaten in a game – that maybe somebody has out-thought you. Absolutely. You’re right to think that.

"[Falkirk manager] John McGlynn has every right to be proud of his side after beating us and to have had the upper hand."

Despite recent form, O'Neill remains more determined than ever to turn things around.

"I'm supposed to be the driver of the club," he quipped.

That said, the 74-year-old is well aware of the threat Besiktas, who sit eighth in the 36-team Europa League table, pose.

"I think these games are really hard for us," O'Neill said. "I think there are teams in the Europa League who are capable of playing in the Champions League and getting results in the Champions League at this minute. However, we're in the Europa League.

"We haven't got a point on the board. We're going to try and do something about it if we can on Thursday and then ask the question.

"But I think that no-one has ever lost the first three and qualified for the competition. So, we’ll be up against it. We're definitely up against it. But are we capable? Yeah, I have to believe that."

#2248. Swansea City receive significant boost as Vitor Matos issues injury update on key defender

Swansea City have hit a poor run of form after a phenomenal start to the season. The Swans were top of the pile after 12 games, but a run of three defeats in four games has seen them drop to 4th, with a midweek defeat to Portsmouth the latest in that run.

During that Pompey clash, summer signing Stephen Welsh went off with a knee problem that appeared serious. However, as they head to Birmingham City this weekend, Vitor Matos has revealed that the injury is not as bad as first thought and he could be in contention to face Chris Davies’ side, as reported by Wales Online.

Matos said: “We will still assess if he will be available for Saturday but it’s not a bad injury. It’s just a knock that is really really painful.”

Welsh has made a great impression since making the switch from Celtic in the summer. Eom Jisung and Moussa Yeo are back available for the Swans this weekend as well, while Ben Cabango is also fit despite a recent knock.

Welsh has quickly established himself as one of Swansea City’s most important players since arriving on a free transfer from Celtic in summer 2026. The 26-year-old signed a two-year contract, with the option of a further year, becoming Vitor Matos’ first summer recruit.

Cameron Burgess’ long-term Achilles injury opened the door for Welsh, who has since made the centre-back position his own. He has started 13 of Swansea’s 14 Championship matches, primarily alongside Ben Cabango, and has impressed with his composure, defensive work and ability to play out from the back.

His passing accuracy, progressive distribution and contribution to Swansea’s defensive organisation have made him an ideal fit for Matos’ possession-based approach. His performances have also earned him his first senior Scotland caps.

With Swansea challenging near the top of the table, Welsh has become a key figure. Securing an experienced defender of his quality on a free transfer is proving to be excellent summer business.

Swansea are short of centre-back options without Welsh, although their defensive depth remains manageable in the short term.

Cabango is the obvious leader, while Jenson Seelt offers the most natural replacement after already featuring under Matos. The Sunderland loanee would be the likely partner for Cabango if Welsh is unavailable.

Filip Lissah provides further cover, having demonstrated his versatility by operating centrally, but regularly relying on him could weaken the right-back position. Cameron Burgess remains sidelined with a long-term Achilles injury, leaving Swansea more exposed if Welsh faces an extended absence.

The Newcastle United academy graduate has enjoyed a fine first 500 games, with 102 of those coming in the Premier League, albeit he would have ultimately liked to have seen a better return than eight goals and nine assists in the top-flight.

#2246. Supercomputer names the 3 EFL League One clubs that will get promoted

We're a quarter of the way through the EFL season, which gives us a good sample size to set more informed predictions about how the campaign will play out in all three divisions.

In League One, Huddersfield Town and Stockport County have been the two sides who have consistently impressed and deservedly led the way in the top two.

But below them, Luton Town have picked up a head of steam and some strong underlying numbers are starting to translate into results. They even took a lead at the Accu Stadium on Tuesday night against the league leaders, but were forced to settle for a draw.

Then, further down the table, Sheffield Wednesday have appointed Dave Challinor, and many believe that's the start of a resurgence at Hillsborough. Leicester City have the quality on the pitch and a system that's starting to settle under Russell Martin, and between them, the likes of Reading, Plymouth Argyle and Oxford United will be competitive, too.

So, it's all shaping up to be a very intriguing promotion race with a lot of sides who will believe they can go up, but who does AI think will be in the Championship next season? Football League World has taken a look to find out.

Winning the league, quite surprisingly in AI's eyes, are Oxford. The surprise mainly comes from the Yellows being nowhere near the top six in many critics' pre-season predictions.

There was logic to that reason, too, as Oxford weren't able to spend money and invest in the squad over the summer due to an embargo imposed on them after failing to comply with the EFL's SCMP regulations.

Football League World takes an in-depth look at the TEN most frustrating EFL Championship players right now

The Yellows were also going into the season with a novice head coach, too, after Matt Bloomfield was sacked in favour of ex-Arsenal and Wales midfielder Aaron Ramsey. Quite bluntly, no one really knew what to expect.

However, Ramsey has more than hit the ground running in the dugout, and Oxford's embargo, despite its frustrations, did mean that the vast majority of their squad that finished 22nd in the Championship and had a mid-table points tally under Bloomfield stuck around. The likes of Mark Harris, Michal Helik and Ciaron Brown have been great.

But also, Ramsey has done well to re-integrate some players many fans felt would never wear an Oxford shirt again. Siriki Dembele has been the main example of that, but Gatlin O'Donkor and Louie Sibley have contributed well, too.

There are questions about the overall depth at the Kassam. Teenagers Mo Missanga and Leo Snowdon are seeing more matchday squads than perhaps expected; albeit Missanga has been brilliant, but hopefully the embargo can be lifted in January, and Ramsey can bring in some reinforcements to help solidify what AI expects to be a title charge.

The current league leaders, Huddersfield, have been forecast by AI to slip from the summit but still secure automatic promotion.

That may be a disappointment right now, but given that the Terriers have failed to even finish in the top six in their two campaigns at third-tier level since their relegation from the Championship, it's a position they should more than take.

There's a clear argument that Huddersfield's squad is already Championship quality. It definitely is at the top end of the pitch. Joe Taylor and Derensili Sanches Fernandes already have 25 goals and assists between them through 14 games, which is quite frankly ridiculous.

But behind them, Cameron Humphreys has been one of the best midfielders in the division for a couple of years now, and it's mental that Huddersfield were able to keep him around for a second loan spell from Ipswich Town, and Archie Collins is more than thriving at a top-end League One club after leaving Peterborough United.

It says a lot that there are question marks over where Ryan Ledson fits in when he comes back from suspension, with the Terriers' club captain and someone with close to 200 Championship appearances patiently waiting on the sidelines.

And it's all being overseen by Martin Drury, an exciting, young manager whose attention to detail and demanding nature of a good performance can ruffle some feathers in the 'only results matter' camp, but it's more than working so far.

There may be a slight drop-off, but one that AI believes shouldn't be too detrimental to their promotion push.

After failing in the play-offs for the last two seasons, AI thinks that Stockport will finally get over the line and secure a second-tier return for the first time since the 2001/02 campaign.

There were some understandable concerns about the immediate future of the Hatters heading into this season, with influential CEO Simon Wilson and manager Dave Challinor moving on after the play-off final, but those have been dispelled very quickly.

There's an argument to be made that County are even better now under Jimmy McNulty than they were under Challinor, and that's no slight on the good work done at Edgeley Park over the previous five years.

They have so many different avenues of attack, whether that be patient, on the floor, working through the lines with Ollie Norwood and a reintegrated Lewis Bate in the pivots, or they can go long and hit Kyle Wootton, who remains one of the best target men strikers in the entire EFL, given how well he can hold the ball up and be a goal threat, too.

Do you agree with AI's promotion predictions in League One this season? Let us know in the comments.

Stockport can sometimes get caught out defensively. It took them a little while to get to grips with their new out-of-possession play at the start of the season, and Oxford did showcase how to beat their high line, once they finally stayed onside.

But any worries of them fading away after back-to-back third-placed finishes seem to have dissipated already, and AI feels that it could be third time's the charm in terms of them earning promotion, winning ahead of the likes of Plymouth, Reading, Sheffield Wednesday, Luton and Leicester.

#2245. Alfred Johansson issues major style warning to his Motherwell side

Motherwell manager Alfred Johansson believes they will need to develop different edges to their game as opponents catch up with their passing style.

Well are second only to Celtic in attempted passes and passing accuracy in the William Hill Premiership this season as they continue the brand of football introduced by Johansson’s predecessor, Jens Berthel Askou, last summer.

But other teams are now catching on after the success Motherwell and Falkirk had last season in finishing in the top six.

Johansson cited recent statistics that showed passes per Premiership game had increased by more than 100 to close to 900 from four seasons ago to this campaign, while passing accuracy was up from 74 per cent to more than 80 per cent and long passes had reduced by around a quarter over the same period.

“I saw some statistics a couple of weeks ago, how much higher the amount of passes in the Scottish football games are this season, compared only with last season,” Johansson said.

“There was also a comparison over a five-year period, and the amount of passes has increased a lot. And it was also a big step from last season to how this season has started.

“The game is moving in that direction, which I guess is natural. I mean, that’s how the trend has been in the rest of European football for quite some time.”

Johansson’s opponent on Saturday, Jim Goodwin, admitted last season that he would aspire to play the sort of football that Askou delivered and his new-look midfield trio of Dylan Tait, Lyall Cameron and Zidane Iqbal have helped adapt their style of play.

United are unbeaten in six matches since losing at Fir Park while Motherwell’s 1-0 win over St Mirren last weekend was their first in eight games.

Johansson senses that Motherwell’s style of play will not give them an inherent advantage.

“It was very different from many of the other teams (last season) and that then gave Motherwell an edge because they were very early,” the Swede said.

“Whereas this season, it’s more teams trying to, maybe not play in the exact same way, but at least have more passes than they had in the past.

“So if that is the case, that’s not going to be the one thing that’s going to be the difference between the teams.

“So I guess that style of play will not be the thing that separates the winner from loser. I think you’re going to have to be finding an edge elsewhere for how good you are with and without the ball.

“You need to be good on your basics. Making minor adaptations to an opponent is always going to be there, but it’s going to be minor changes.”

#2244. Middlesbrough fans think Kim Hellberg got one thing wrong v Wrexham

Middlesbrough fell to their second defeat of the Championship season on Wednesday night, as Wrexham bounced back from consecutive defeats to pick up a vital three points at the Racecourse Ground.

In a game with very few chances, Phil Parkinson's side broke the deadlock with 20 minutes to go through George Thomason, and made sure the points were safe after Ollie Rathbone scored a second six minutes from time.

It's the first time that Middlesbrough have failed to score all season. Kim Hellberg's side have been electric going forward, led primarily by Will Lankshear, and it wasn't a coincidence that his absence after suffering a muscle issue led to Boro being a little less dangerous in front of goal.

However, it wasn't just the fact that Middlesbrough lined up with the likes of Kyle Joseph, Tommy Conway and David Strelec as the central nine at times on Wednesday night which frustrated Boro fans, as for the first time in quite a while, Hellberg himself wasn't without criticism.

Kim Hellberg is one of the more tactically fluid head coaches in the Championship today. He's comfortable deploying a back four or a back three, and spoke in his press conference last week about being happy operating with two strikers.

Unfortunately, the switch to a back three in possession and a back five out of it against Wrexham on Wednesday night has drawn quite a bit of criticism from Boro fans.

With Hellberg's side having a chance to extend their lead atop the table to four points last night against a Wrexham side who have underwhelmed this season and had lost their last two, the supporters were disappointed to see a perceived 'negative' lineup.

"Far too negative against a bang average side Kim. I hope you've learned from this? You know the score mate, sort it out. UTB!🇦🇹" one fan said on X in response to the Swede's post-match interview.

"5 at the back? More defensive against Moore? A team sat in the bottom half of the league, shambles Kim," another said.

Do you think Kim Hellberg got it wrong against Wrexham on Wednesday? Let us know in the comments.

Other fans accused Hellberg of "inviting Wrexham to give us a game," while others expressed frustration at setting up in a five "against a poor team." "He has to take responsibility for the formation and selections!" was also among the comments.

"Lost for words Kim? Poor team selection and formation tonight. Never play with a back 3 again," was said bluntly. Evidently, the majority of Middlesbrough fans are hoping Hellberg reverts to a back four on Saturday at Watford.

Ultimately, while Wrexham haven't enjoyed the start to the season that many expected they would have, Phil Parkinson's side is still full of talented players who can hurt any team on a good day.

This one can be chalked up to a sub-par performance from a Middlesbrough side and a bit of a better one from a Wrexham perspective, and the fact that Boro are still sitting top of the division regardless, just shows how strong they have been to start the season.

No league champion goes the whole campaign without losing, and those at the Riverside will be hoping that this is just a mere bump in the road en route to a Premier League return.

Kim Hellberg's side are in action at Vicarage Road on Saturday as they take on Watford in the final game before the November international break.

#2241. Spurs will be surprised but Leicester's won't by Oliver Skipp at Charlton

Having shone when coming through the academy at Tottenham Hotspur and then experienced a bright start to his career in the EFL, Oliver Skipp is again struggling this season, now at Charlton Athletic after a tough time of it at Leicester City.

Following their promotion back to the Championship via the 2024-25 League One play-offs, Charlton have looked to assert themselves as a part of the furniture again in the second-tier, under the management of Nathan Jones.

A young squad under Jones as they gained promotion, Charlton did have a fair bit of experience dotted around their squad but, as ever with a team heading into a campaign seeking to avoid ‘second season syndrome’, they have again sought to add more pedigree to their squad.

That may well have even been with a view to kicking on into the middle-of-the-table and, albeit as an outsider, looked at the top eight and the expanded play-off spots to eight teams in the Championship this season.

One player that was brought in with good pedigree to provide extra quality in the middle of the park was Oliver Skipp – but the 26-year-old is enduring another struggle this season down at The Valley.

Over the summer, Charlton added a fair bit of experience to the middle of the park with the signing of Nathaniel Chalobah on a free transfer from Sheffield Wednesday and Timothy Ouma from Slavia Prague.

Both defensive midfielders, it was somewhat surprising that The Addicks then signed Oliver Skipp on loan from Leicester City on the final day of the campaign – albeit in a Nathan Jones-coached side, perhaps you can’t have too many conservative and defensive-minded midfielders.

Skipp, Ouma and Chalobah joined a squad that already boasted other key midfielders such as Conor Coventry, Greg Docherty and the slightly more advanced Sonny Carey, while the versatile Joe Rankin-Costello is also capable of playing in a central midfield role.

Whether it be a 4-2-3-1 system or even a three-at-the-back with either one or two so-called ‘number tens’; there tends to only be room for two out and out box-to-box or defensive central midfielders – and with the quality Skipp has previously shown and the experience he already has in his mid-20’s; Skipp was viewed as a floor-raising addition to a Charlton side that had won their opening three games of the campaign before his arrival.

However, Charlton have since gone on a ten-match winless run of form after the arrival of Skipp and his own individual performance have perhaps been reflective of the malaise and regression of the team as a whole.

A look at seven clubs who should consider trying to sign former Tottenham Hotspur and England man Dele Alli.

The former England U21 international came into the side immediately and played the full 90 minutes as Charlton endured their first loss of the season – a 4-0 hammering at the hands of Stoke City.

He has gone on to start seven of the next eight matches, too, only not involved against Norwich City after being hooked at half-time in a 2-0 defeat to Watford a week earlier.

He came back into the eleven against Middlesbrough but found himself in a midfield that was completely overrun by the league leaders in a 3-0 defeat at the Riverside Stadium in that one.

Keeping out Coventry in midfield, as well as even Chalobah, who had started life in South London well, being moved to the periphery of things seems now to be a surprising inevitability for Skipp.

Once a metronomic figure in terms of his passing and tempo setting when coming through Tottenham youth teams and then on loan at Norwich; Skipp has badly struggled to adapt to the more conservative nature of Jones’ style.

Skipp has remained tidy enough on the ball with an 83.1% pass success rate but he is averaging too few passes per game and allowing games to pass him by, averaging more than 12 passes per 90 fewer than Conor Coventry, as an example.

Previously better known for his ability on the ball, it is his defensive work that has been his more impressive suit thus far this season but, with ball-winners such as Chalobah at the club, more quality was to be expected of the Leicester loanee.

That quality was shown when coming through at Spurs as he made the fringes of the first-team picture in North London and even began to emerge with more regular minutes when coached by Antonio Conte – who he once suggested he could help become a ‘top midfielder’.

His breakthrough into more regular minutes at Spurs had come after an extremely impressive loan stint at Norwich during the 2020-21 campaign – named in the PFA Team of the Year for the second division that season as The Canaries won the Championship title under Daniel Farke.

That season, Skipp was that metronome in Norwich’s proactive and entertaining side, averaging 51.5 passes per game with an 88% pass success rate – dwarfing his figures at Charlton so far this season and highlighting his drop off.

That drop off being seen by Charlton supporters this season may come as a surprise to Spurs and Norwich supporters but it won’t be much of a shock for Leicester fans.

Purchased by The Foxes in the summer of 2024 for a fee believed to be in the region of £20 million, following Leicester’s Championship title-winning campaign under Enzo Maresca, Skipp linked up with Steve Cooper at the King Power Stadium.

A good coach but a pragmatic one, the passing and ability to retain possession that had been qualities of Skipp weren’t necessarily going to be used too much for a Cooper-led relegation battler.

However, his fierceness in the tackle and general industry also diminished in a Leicester squad that succumbed to back-to-back relegations from the Premier League and into League One.

Perhaps a very good example of Leicester’s issues in the last half a decade, being a big money signing brought in without too much sense in terms of exactly what they wanted; Skipp badly struggled at the King Power Stadium.

Having been one of the best players in the Championship and then someone seemingly coming close to England recognition in the years before his arrival at Leicester; a loss of confidence and inability to rediscover his quality on the ball led to Skipp sticking out as a fairly hapless figure in Leicester’s badly underperforming side.

Brought in for such a big fee, he managed just ten starts in his debut campaign as they were relegated from the Premier League before being a peripheral figure in the opening few months of last season.

Skipp became a key man for a Leicester side that became enemies of the fanbase and his failure to rediscover any sort of form at Charlton thus far will not come as much of a surprise to Foxes supporters.

#2239. Birmingham City confirm major boost ahead of Swansea City clash

Birmingham City's preparations for this weekend's mouth-watering clash with Swansea City at St. Andrew's have been boosted by Paik Seung-ho's return to training at EPIC.

Blues have won four of their last five Championship matches and sit third in the table ahead of the final round of fixtures before the two-week international break, with Swansea only two places below on goal difference.

Davies isn't exactly short of options at this moment in time, although James Beadle and August Priske were omitted from the matchday squad against Millwall through injury.

Paik had been a mainstay in Davies' side for much of his tenure, having arrived just months before the 41-year-old was appointed two years ago, signing from Jeonbuk Hyundai Motors in January 2024.

2026 has been a tale of frustration for the South Korean though. Having missed a portion of last season with injury misfortune, he hasn't been able to aid Birmingham's impressive start this time around for similar reasons.

The 30-time South Korean international has featured 10 times in all competitions this season, albeit only four of his eight Championship outings have come from the starting lineup.

The last of those came through a seven-minute cameo in the 2-2 draw with Middlesbrough before the extended first international break of the season.

Paik has missed the past seven Championship games after the South Korean Football Association released a statement on the decision to return him to the West Midlands club for "protection."

Earlier this week, Davies revealed the midfielder could be involved in some capacity, and images have now surfaced of the 29-year-old partaking in full training with his teammates ahead of the pivotal weekend showdown.

The former Barcelona academy graduate has also become a mainstay for his national side, featuring three times at the FIFA World Cup in the United States, Canada and Mexico, which landed Birmingham a cash windfall.

However, the Second City outfit will be somewhat relieved to have learned of another scenario involving Paik which emerged this week, even if it isn't the best news for his nation.

South Korea were one of the nations set to be involved in the Asia Cup in early 2027, but the ongoing Middle East conflict has seen the tournament postponed until 13 months' time.

The summer recruit was key to Blues' fourth win in five Championship games

Max Bird was moved into the '10' against Millwall, a decision which paid off with a goal and assist for the summer recruit, but he could return to a deeper role if Priske is deemed fit due to Jay Stansfield's recent form in the attacking-midfield role.

Paik could still come off the bench and play a key role though, particularly if Blues need to see the game out against Matos' side.

#2238. Birmingham City told to strike Nottingham Forest transfer for 95-cap international star

Birmingham City's impressive start to the season has got many fans at St. Andrew's @ Knighthead Park dreaming of a return to the Premier League for the first time since 2011.

Chris Davies' side have only lost two of their first 14 matches of the Championship season, winning four of their last five and scoring 14 goals in the process.

Tuesday's controlled 3-0 win over Millwall saw Birmingham reach the automatic promotion places for just over 24 hours, before they were knocked down to third for the time being after Wolves' win at Bristol City.

Such a strong start wasn't exactly forecast by many in this part of the Second City at the beginning of the season, as it was argued that the squad was left short in certain areas.

However, given the well-documented ambitions of American investors, Knighthead Capital Management, many are now debating what activity will be conducted in the winter window if Blues are still among the promotion contenders.

With Birmingham's forward department currently made up of August Priske, Jay Stansfield, Luis Vazquez and Marvin Ducksch, it has been questioned what changes will be made going forward.

Priske has already netted six times this season whilst Stansfield has now firmly aclimatised to playing as a 'number 10', whilst exit talk continues to surround Ducksch after a failed summer departure to Valencia, and Vazquez is yet to convince the majority of supporters.

As such, it was recently debated by Blues YouTuber, Tommy Kelsall, whether a reunion for Chris Wood, who scored 11 times in 29 appearances on loan in the 2011/12 season under Chris Hughton, makes sense, given his similarities with Priske, but also the experience under his belt.

The in-form striker was absent from Tuesday's dominant success over Millwall

Wood has only made two Premier League starts under Oliver Glasner at Nottingham Forest this season, having seen his 2025/26 campaign blighted by a knee injury.

Beforehand, the 95-time New Zealand international scored 20 times as the Reds secured European football in 2024/25, and his record of 170 strikes in the top two divisions is indisputable.

With his contract at the City Ground up next June, Football League World's Birmingham City fan pundit, Jason Moore, believes the 34-year-old would be a great option for Davies to call upon.

"I don't mind the shout," Moore told FLW.

"People might say he's aging, but he's, again, a proven Premier League striker. He's done a decent job in the Premier League.

"He's not pacey. But, I don't think that's his game and he'd probably be, like that tweet says, a like-for-like for August Priske in the way we play, so I'd be all up for it.

"You know he's going to work hard, he'd take that pressure off Priske playing every single game, and he's a miles better replacement than Luis Vazquez.

"If you can look at Chris Wood and if he's not playing at Forest, then I'd be all for it. If we could pull that off, that's a great shout. Him or Che (Adams), I'd be all up for it."

Blues fans, should Chris Davies attempt to sign Chris Wood in January? Comment below!

"I'd narrowly favour Che for his pace, but I'd be more than happy with Chris Wood if Che wasn't available. Both are excellent options."

Having a focal point in attack such as Priske has allowed Birmingham to source goals from across Davies' squad, and whilst the Dane has become a feared striker this term, Wood's experience would certainly aid a promotion bid.

Like Priske, he has the ability to bring fellow attackers into the game and make their own deep runs into the box and create chances, whilst also having a poacher's instinct despite not being particularly fast.

If Birmingham were also to end their Premier League exile, then a player of such calibre would also be worth retaining, given Wood's extensive record in both divisions.

The nature of the Championship schedule also means that the forward is likely to source more minutes in the West Midlands than he currently is in the East.

"I would prefer it if he stayed under the radar a little bit and stayed with Sheffield United. "We've been in this position before when we sold David Brooks, and we brought in players like John Egan and Ollie Norwood. But what is that price? I think it's at least £15 million. When you look at some of the prices that players are going for at this level now, he's an England U21 international who just scored a hat-trick in the Championship; at least £15 million. I'd be holding out for £20 million."

Do you agree with Jimmy's take on Sydie Peck? How much do you think Sheffield United should be commanding for the midfielder if interest were to arrive as early as January? Share your thoughts below!

Having been forced to part ways with Brooks, Hamer, and O'Hare in the summer, Sheffield United and their head coach Chris Wilder will have little intention of losing any other stars anytime soon.

Peck is certainly one of their prized assets right now, and under contract until the summer of 2028, are under no immediate pressure to cash in, although, a decision on his future must be made sooner rather than later.

The England youth international's ceiling is extraordinarily high, and Sheffield United will be hoping to be the continued beneficiaries of his services for years to come, as time will tell exactly how this saga plays out.

#2233. Manchester United player ratings: United failed to win despite advantage

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United and Tottenham Hotspur returned from the international break with plenty to prove after a disastrous start to the season.

United arrived with just one win, while Tottenham came to Old Trafford without a win.

Both teams had half-chances in the first half but little to show for them. Tottenham had the better chances with chances for Dominic Solanke, Connor Gallagher and Kudus, but none could take advantage of them.

Manchester United came close in the first half to score after Bryan Mbeumo raced towards the goal on a counter-attack to find Amad. He had a difficult shot, and eventually the shot was too weak to cause any damage. The first-half display was met by boos from the Old Trafford crowd at half-time and during the first half as well.

Solanke had another chance at the start of the second half but put it over the top, amid a wrestle with Lisandro Martinez in the six-yard box. It was United, however, who started the second half with promise.

Substitutions by Michael Carrick around the hour mark made the difference as United showed intent before Mbeumo got the goal off Cunha’s low cross. Luke Shaw was at the end of a scuffle with Bentancur, who was shown a red card for headbutting Shaw.

United failed to capitalize on their one-man advantage and seemed to sit on their 1-0 lead, but Solanke punished them with a header to level the game in injury time. The draw left United with just 6 points off their first six games. The result leaves United with their worst start in 40 years.

Lenne Sammens-6          Quiet afternoon for United no.1 till the goal, where he saw the ball go into the back of the net.

Leny Yoro-6       Defensively was fine but offered little upfront. Played out of position, and he showed good defensive awareness on the right back till his substitution.

Harry Maguire-5.5            Was decent in the first half but wasn’t much of an answer to Tottenham going forward in the second half

Lisandro Martinez-5      Took his eyes off the ball, resulting in conceding a late goal off Solanke.

Luke Shaw-6      Went up high on the pitch and remained a threat with his overlapping threat. Was involved in the scuffle that got Bentancur a red card for headbutting.

Kobbie Mainoo-6.5        Brilliant defensive tackle to deny Tottenham in the 68th minute. Remained composed in tight spaces against a physical Tottenham midfield.

Youri Tielemans-6          A decent performance and was solid throughout the minutes he was on the field

Bruno Fernandes-5        Another quiet and disappointing evening for the United captain after he failed to impress on a day he needed to get a result for United.

Amad-6              A positive return from injury, showed plenty of promise on the wing but failed to get past a resolute Tottenham side

Matheus Cunha-7           One of the liveliest options for United going forward. Provided an assist to Mbeumo to put United up front

Bryan Mbeumo-7.5        A brilliant finish off Matheus Cunha’s low ball to put United in front. Was dangerous going forward and denied a second on a counter by Kinsky off a brilliant long ball by Baleba.

Carlos Baleba (58’)-6.5  A decent debut overall for the Cameroon international. He put in a couple of dangerous balls up the field, including a clear ball to Mbeumo on a counter, who was denied by Kinsky.

Diogo Dalot (58’)-6      Provided a decent attacking threat when brought on around the hour mark.

Joshua Zirkzee (58’)-6   Showed plenty of promise and was a threat after he was brought in around the hour mark.

#2232. Scott Parker has been sent £18m West Ham message and Spurs are laughing loudest

Scott Parker’s start to life as West Ham head coach got off to a thrilling start.

The Hammers drew 3-3 against Cardiff City on Wednesday night in his first game in the dugout, with the team coming from 3-0 behind to secure a point on the road.

Goals from Divine Mukasa, Arne Engels and Max Kilman sealed an exciting comeback in the second half, with the London outfit hoping that they can compete for automatic promotion this season.

But Parker’s first game as manager showed that there is perhaps more work to be done at the London Stadium than initially thought.

The first issue that Parker may have to address as the new West Ham boss is the underwhelming form of summer arrival Manor Solomon.

The winger was brought in with the expectation that he could power the team to promotion, much like he did at Leeds United in 2024/25, where he contributed 10 goals and 12 assists in 39 Championship appearances.

Should Scott Parker drop Manor Solomon? Comment your opinion below

But his form has been inconsistent at the London Stadium so far, and another underwhelming performance on Wednesday night led to him being hauled off after 69 minutes.

While he managed to complete 13 of his 15 attempted passes, the overall total matches that of someone quite uninvolved in the game, and he also failed to create a chance or take a clear shot at goal, leading to a deserved substitution (all stats from Fotmob).

Edvin Austbo was chosen to replace Solomon for the final 20 minutes against Cardiff, making his 10th appearance in the league this season.

This was the eighth time he came off the bench, and his lively performance helped the team in their comeback from 3-0 down.

West Ham United considered the potential appointment of former Tottenham Hotspur and Liverpool coach Johnny Heitinga before deciding on Scott Parker

The Norwegian already has as many goals and assists as Solomon so far this campaign, despite playing only 347 minutes compared to 1,003.

The 21-year-old arrived from Viking IF in a deal worth £18 million in the summer, and now could be the time for Parker to give him an extended run in the team ahead of Solomon.

Austbo hasn’t had an extended run in the team, which impacts how we can judge him, but his appearances so far have shown a lot of promise.

The winger has an expected assists (xA) per 90 of 0.53, creating 0.53 big chances per 90.

He also has 1.32 successful crosses each game at a success rate of 35.7 per cent, which makes him a dangerous threat in the final third, all while also taking 2.63 shots each game himself.

If he can replicate these kinds of performances over a series of starts in the coming weeks, then Parker will have no choice but to keep him in the lineup going forward over Solomon.

West Ham’s rivals Tottenham Hotspur signed Solomon in 2023, but he struggled for game time under various managers at the London outfit, which led to loan spells at Leeds, Villarreal and Fiorentina.

But the Hammers committed to a £5 million deal to sign the winger despite his lack of impact at Spurs.

Given the question marks over his quality now, the Premier League side have had the last laugh on their rival.

Parker dropping him to the bench would be the ultimate vindication for Spurs’ decision to cash in on the forward last summer.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

#2231. Michael Carrick reacts to Man Utd boos after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick admitted Manchester United’s 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur was a painful result, after his side failed to protect the lead against 10-man Tottenham at Old Trafford.

Manchester United looked set to claim three points when Bryan Mbeumo scored in the second half off Cunha’s low cross, but Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser in injury time left Carrick facing more questions on United’s performance and his future.

Speaking to BBC Match of the Day, the United manager felt his players improved after the break but failed to handle the situation when victory was within reach.

“It was a mixed game. We found it tough to start with. For a number of reasons, we were not quite there. You could see it was a challenging time,” Carrick said.

“But the lads grew into the game, in the second half I thought we were better in everything. To get the game as we wanted it, in such a good position – we should see it through. We have to manage those situations.”

United have now conceded two late goals that have cost them valuable points, something Carrick believes has made their position look worse than it might have otherwise.

Asked whether a lack of confidence had contributed to United’s slow start, he disagreed but admitted matches can quickly slip away from his side.

The boos at half-time and full-time also clearly affected him.

“It’s not nice, it hurts a lot because I care about this club. But I understand the disappointment, the reality is what it is, we need to get back to winning games.”

#2230. Barella admits Inter have changed derby approach in recent years: “We’re too inconsistent”

After the game, Nicolo Barella attempted to lessen the damage of last night’s loss to AC Milan in his post-game press conference.

For Milan, last night was another instance in what is becoming a new norm. After Inter’s streak of derby wins, it’s now the Rossoneri who are enjoying themselves. They’ve won each of their last four meetings and haven’t lost in eight games.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

In short, despite all of the managerial changes that have taken place, the Diavolo remain on top when it comes to playing the Nerazzurri.

Of course, last night’s win was also monumental considering what it meant for the Serie A table. Rather than falling 10 points behind, they only sit four points behind Inter and Lazio, who share first.

Speaking at a post-match conference, Barella admitted that Inter are going through a bad cycle in the derbies, but was keen to remember the streak from a few years ago, as MilanNews relayed.

“Certainly, if we talk about this, we’ve been lacking the ideal approach to matches since the start of the year. Right now, it’s a clear criticality for us.

“It’s true that we’ve shown our strength on many occasions, perhaps coming out on top in the second half or whenever we manage to break the deadlock, but it’s clear that not every match goes that way.

“We can’t expect to always repeat the same pattern, especially when we face a packed schedule with matches every three days. Fatigue, both physical and mental, is starting to take its toll, and we need to learn to manage matches better without being forced to keep them in the balance until the ninetieth minute.”

Is there a feeling that, whatever you do, Milan will prevail in derbies?

“Let’s say I’ve also experienced it the other way around. I’ve had a lot of derby wins in a row, so I don’t know if it’s a cyclical thing, but it’s definitely a game we haven’t approached in the best way in recent years, recently.

“There have been derbies where we’ve played well and lost, where we’ve played poorly and maybe drawn, but we’re too inconsistent in derbies, and right now, even in important matches. Even though we won, we still needed to do better.”

Do you still have the fierce desire to prove that you are the strongest?

“Being the strongest is sometimes not enough, because we’ve also been the strongest team, or the one everyone thought was the strongest, but then we didn’t win. So right now, I think we need to focus on something else rather than our strength.

“It’s true that if we look back at the last few games, many goals come from individual errors, and we need to improve on those, each of us, because unfortunately, individual errors work against the team. But as a team, we need to improve a bit in terms of solidity, which is what has always set us apart, what got us to the Champions League final, what helped us win the Scudetto, and perhaps right now we’re not the best version of ourselves. So we need to work on that. We still have the desire to win, for sure, because that’s what keeps us going to the pitch every day and motivates us, but we need to improve.”

#2229. Bryan Mbeumo reacts to Man Utd draw with Tottenham

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Bryan Mbeumo admitted Manchester United have work to do after their frustrating 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, despite scoring a superb second-half goal at Old Trafford.

The Cameroon international put Manchester United ahead in the 73rd minute, finishing in the far corner after Matheus Cunha created the opportunity from a low cross. However, Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory, even though Tottenham had been reduced to 10 men following Rodrigo Bentancur’s red card.

Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2).

3 – Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2). Disadvantage? pic.twitter.com/DuSpvymZfu

Speaking to Sky Sports after the game, Mbeumo acknowledged that United had taken too long to find their rhythm.

“We took time to get into the game, but we played well in the second half. In the first half, that wasn’t us, we had to change. We tried to get at them,” he said.

Mbeumo recognised the threat Tottenham posed and admitted the result was disappointing, particularly given United’s position in the game before Solanke’s late goal.

“We know Spurs have good players who can be dangerous. It’s a bit disappointing, we need to work on things.”

United have struggled for consistency in the opening weeks of the season, and dropping points after taking the lead will do little to ease the pressure on Carrick. Mbeumo, however, insisted the team must look ahead rather than dwell on the setback.

“We need to keep going forward, as we have a lot of games.”

Michael Carrick was pleased with Mbeumo’s contribution, highlighting both the quality of his finish and the different roles he has played since joining United.

“Bryan’s done great. It was a fantastic finish again; he’s obviously played different roles for us but we know what he offers,” the United manager said.

“He’s got good quality and obviously the more goals he scores, the better. He’s doing really well for us.”

Mbeumo’s goal was one of the few bright moments in another difficult afternoon for United. Carrick’s team must now find a way to turn things around from here after just six points from six games.

#2228. Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp tear into Man Utd after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have come under fresh criticism after their disappointing 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, with Sky Sports pundits Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp questioning the team’s performance and the challenges facing Michael Carrick.

Manchester United looked on course for a much-needed victory at Old Trafford after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half. However, despite Tottenham being reduced to 10 men, Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser denied Carrick’s side all three points.

Gary Neville felt Tottenham deserved the points, pointing to Solanke’s late goal and his impact as Tottenham captain.

“Tottenham deserved a point. That was a really important moment for Solanke. He’d missed some big chances and was captain for the first time,” Neville said on Sky Sports.

“They were the better team by far. If they’d left without a point, it would have been terrible for them.”

The former United defender also expressed concern about what the result means for Carrick, who faces increasing pressure to improve performances and results.

“Michael Carrick is left with a lot to think about. It was a worrying day in terms of the level of performance and a big week ahead.”

Manchester United spent massively in the summer to overhaul their midfield. However, Jamie Redknapp was critical of the performance he saw on the pitch.

“I do not see enough connections in that midfield, and they look devoid of any confidence, ideas, or patterns of play, playing out the back,” Redknapp said.

“Tottenham were just much better than them in every department, but that is a theme this season.”

The former Liverpool midfielder also questioned United’s energy and attacking organization, suggesting the squad is not strong enough to meet the demands placed upon it.

“Their energy levels are not good enough, and they look loose going forward; there was no real structure.”

Redknapp added that Carrick cannot fix every issue alone, insisting the manager needs backing to improve the squad.

“The squad is not good or strong enough, and Michael [Carrick] needs help in that respect.”

#2227. Ryan Jack knew Clement was doomed at Rangers as he reveals bad taste in his mouth

A Tuesday night in May and Rangers are hosting Dundee in their last home league game of the season. After seven years, one league title, a Scottish Cup medal and a European final, Ryan Jack is preparing to say farewell to Ibrox.

“I took along all my family that night thinking, ‘it might be my last home game,” he tells Herald Sport.

“I had been to the hotel all day with the team all days and had lunch and a sleep before the bus to Ibrox.

“We got to the stadium pre-match and nobody has said a word to me, so I am thinking I am in the squad and might play…”

Any realistic hope of Rangers winning the league title had extinguished the previous week after a defeat at Parkhead.

Fully recovered from a muscle injury, Jack still had plenty to play for. His contract was up at the end of the season and the Scottish Cup Final against Celtic was just around the corner. Despite injuries corrupting his season he felt sure he still had something to offer.

He had no idea if Philippe Clement felt the same. Whenever he knocked on the manager’s door pinning the Belgian down was like nailing jelly to the wall.

When his name was absent from the squad list for Dundee, he finally had his answer. At 32, he had been around football long enough to sense which way the wind was blowing.

“That was after seven years of graft and putting my life into every day and playing through pain barriers, because that’s what you do.

“That night against Dundee was when I thought that it was not going to work out for Clement at Rangers.

“Because when other people see you treat players who had been there for seven years like that then they say, ‘nah, he’s miles off it.’

“Players are human and it’s like any walk of life. If your boss does that you think, ‘nah, that’s not on.’

Currently a free agent after two years in Turkey, the former Scotland midfielder is taking his time over what comes next. At 34 he wants to play on after the kind of family Christmas he never managed when the festive fixture card piled up one game after another at Aberdeen and Rangers. When he sits down to write some cards to family and friends its safe to assume that the name of the current Norwich City manager won’t be on the list.

“I actually remember guys like Jack Butland, Conor Goldson and James Tavernier and John Lundstram saying to me, ‘that’s an absolute disgrace what the manager has done to you.’

“When that happened the penny dropped that I was definitely going to be leaving for sure.”

For long periods of season 2023/24 Rangers had looked a reasonable bet for the title. Four points adrift of Brendan Rodgers’ Celtic the Ibrox side had the saving grace of two games in hand until they threw the advantage away. They lost the first game in Dingwall to Ross County then failed to find a way through against Dundee in a goalless draw at Dens Park.

By the time they turned around a two goal deficit against the Dark Blues in their final Ibrox home game it was too little, too late. Celtic rattled five goals past Kilmarnock the following night and won their 12th championship in 13 seasons and faith in Clement was draining fast.

An unused substitute as Celtic won the Scottish Cup final with a late Adam Idah goal, Jack delayed his holiday plans to hang back in Glasgow and prepare for a trip to the Euros with Scotland. Despite asking the question - repeatedly - he still had no idea what the future held.

“There were talks ongoing for months and months. It looked like a deal might be happening, then it wasn’t happening.

“You know what it’s like. Managers start having a certain way of communicating with you.

“I went to see him two or three times to say, ‘okay what’s happening here?’

“I wanted to plan for the future and every time it was, ‘oh, we don’t know yet, the club has not made a decision. It’s not my decision.’

“All I wanted was an honest answer. I felt that, when I was fit and well I was still capable of doing a job for sure.

“There were just certain things that made me think my time was coming to an end.”.

As it turned out his last appearance for Rangers came in a 2-2 draw against Benfica in the last 16 of the Europa League in Lisbon in March. A late replacement for Tom Lawrence in the Stadium of Light, a muscle injury ruled him out until he returned to fitness at the end of the season. Even then he didn’t play.

His surprise inclusion in Steve Clarke’s Scotland squad for the Euros in Germany was a reward for his dogged loyalty and performances at international level. He felt none of that payback coming from Philippe Clement.

“I was training for a couple of weeks after the season finished because I wanted to keep ticking over for Euros.

“It was then that he (Clement) phoned me and asked for 10 minutes to speak to me.

“He came in to the training centre and he said that the club had made a decision to go younger and I had suffered a few injuries over my time and that hadn’t helped me with the people above who had doubts.

“He said they wanted to go down a young route and get most of the experienced players out.

“And my comeback to him was, ‘do you think that is the best way to go?’

“A club like Rangers can not just sign a team of players who are 20 or 21 or 22 and expect them to be ready for the expectation and the pressure and the European stage.

“I’ve always felt that, to be successful at Rangers, you need a core who can be around the changing room and lead it. Even if they don’t play every week.

“But he wanted to go young and it didn’t turn out too well for him.”

“That time under Clement left a bad taste - the way he was going about things at the end.”

Jack had known nothing beyond the narrow horizons of Scottish football. Joining Aberdeen’s pro youth system at the age of eight, he rose to become captain of his hometown club. When he took advantage of freedom of contract to sign a three year deal with Rangers in 2017 some took his choice of club badly. His parents’ home address in Aberdeen was posted online accompanied by brainless threats.

“You know what? I grew up a Rangers fan as a young boy and the move wasn’t difficult for me personally.

“I know that the clubs are huge rivals and people take that personally, but I still hold Aberdeen in the highest regard. There was no ill feeling towards the club at all.

“Making the move was always a football decision to go to a new level and play in front of 50,000 people in Europe with a better training facility at the time.

“Off the pitch it brought a lot of stuff and hype media wise, of course it did.

“I’m not going to lie, I felt that pressure when I went back to Pittodrie. Everyone is against you.

“My family stay in the north east and they probably got a bit more of that than I did.

“There were maybe people at Aberdeen who looked at me differently for moving to Rangers, I don’t know.”

One of those who took news of the move to Ibrox badly, ironically, was his Aberdeen manager at the time. One Derek McInnes esq.

“Derek told me that after we played Rangers Pedro Caixinha was in giving it, ‘were going to take your players.’

“Derek had a wee chat with me asking what I was planning. He gave me the captaincy at the club and wanted to build the team around me.

“I loved my time with Derek. Working with him was one of the most enjoyable periods of my career.

“We had a wee bit of a fall out towards the end of the season because he was desperate for me to stay with Aberdeen.

“I wanted to move on and develop my career away from the club, but we are brand new now.”

There was a time, shortly after his own arrival at Ibrox in 2017, when it seemed that McInnes would join him in Glasgow. Jack was told by former Aberdeen teammates that the deal was done until a late u-turn.

McInnes stayed put at Pittodrie, only leaving when he was dismissed in 2021. He found a route to Ibrox eventually - via Kilmarnock and Hearts - while the club which fired him goes through managers like Donald Trump sweats his way through MAGA caps.

Involved with Aberdeen for 17 years Jack thinks the decision to part company with McInnes was a mistake.

“Derek was always made for being at a big club and having that demand on him.

“He knows the league and he knows how to get the job done. And when I look at Aberdeen they have never been able to replace him.

“I think back to the team he assembled when I was there, with Kenny McLean, Adam Rooney, Graeme Shinnie, Jonny Hayes, Niall McGinn, Mark Reynolds, Andy Considine, Shay Logan. Even James Maddison came in on loan for a time.

“We had great characters and we were always finishing second in the league and getting to semi finals and finals every year and yet being captain the whole vibe outside was, ‘we’re not good enough, we cannot compete.’ It was always negative.

“But we were up against Brendan Rodgers and a strong Celtic squad.

“And I don’t think that Aberdeen team that we had will be matched for years to come.

“I don’t know what direction the club wants to go in now or what kind of recruitment they go for.

“It’s mix and match, here, there and everywhere. Managers go from Stephen Glass to Jim Goodwin and Neil Warnock to Stephen Robinson. Yet when I look back at my time with Del it was so settled.”

After two seasons with Turkish club Esenler Erokspor Jack is enjoying some down time back in Scotland. His relationship with McInnes is back on a firm footing and he uses the gym facilities at Auchenhowie to keep fit while he waits for the right offer to get back playing. He is open minded on what comes next.

“I want to play on. I am only 34 and I still feel that I have a bit to give.

“There is no real plan, but I have had a few discussions here and there at a decent enough level with some teams. I want to wait and see what comes up, it needs to be the right place at the right time.

“I am not arrogant about where I play or what I want to do, I have had a good career so far. But I want something that excites me and gives me something to look forward to.”

When the news broke that Jack was using Rangers facilities to keep fit, a post from his former manager Steven Gerrard in Instagram almost broke the internet.

“Put him in the middle,” wrote the England legend. “Proper player.”

“I think that ship has sailed,” says Jack, with a laugh. “I loved my time at the club and have some amazing memories.

“I think Stevie had just seen that I had been training in there and it was a tongue in cheek comment.

“I have said before that the time under Stevie Gerrard was the best football I played in my career.

“Everything he brought when he came in opened my eyes to a new world.”

By  his own admission, he made the most of the talent he had. If anything there were times, under Gerrard, when he pushed his body farther than harder than he should have and it hurt him.

“If I could do more I would do it. If I could train harder I would train harder. If I could run more in a game I did it.

“That’s what the gaffer loved about me. I would never say no.

“I would never say, ‘I’m not training’ or ‘i’m not playing.’

“And that maybe cost me a few injuries over the years.”

Clubs looking for an old head with a ferocious work rate should know that he now feels fit and ready to go. He wants to play while preparing for the next phase.

“It has always fascinated me, managing and having your own team. I want to go down and avenue and see where that leads me…”
